Do not land this version. The publishing path works, but the table’s central assurance claim does not.

## Blocking findings

1. The `record/controls/` claims exemption is indefensible.

The exemption covers the entire directory, including any future hand-written file, not merely the generated artifact ([check_claims.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/check_claims.py:62)). More importantly, scanning the generated Markdown directly finds 17 candidate spans; 16 are undispositioned. Some are not harmless generator churn—the scan exposes materially questionable claims such as:

- “no refused proposal has yet been re-proposed”;
- “nothing here drifts across cycles”;
- “no rollback mechanism exists.”

The first two conflict with the repository’s own determinations ([self_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/self_application.py:83), [self_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/self_application.py:188)). This is exactly the case where the prose gate adds value.

Ruling: remove the skip and take alternative (i), but do not rubber-stamp all spans. Correct or narrow the false claims first, then disposition the remaining generated claims. Changing a derived number changes the factual assertion; invalidating its content-keyed disposition is appropriate review pressure.

Neither alternative (ii) nor (iii) is acceptable: moving the durable record to `docs/` hides the problem, while deleting the caveats makes the artifact materially worse.

2. The checkbox is not derived in the sense claimed.

The actual predicate is only:

```python
scope == CODE and files and tests and not gap and paths_exist
```

([control_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/control_application.py:423)). Whether “every code change” is finished remains encoded manually by omitting `gap`. Yet the page says nothing about the tick is asserted by hand and defines it as every required change being made ([control_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/control_application.py:527)).

That is a category error. The checkbox is a producer declaration with mechanically checked prerequisites.

Represent it honestly as:

- explicit `declared_complete: true`;
- mechanical `substantiated`: referenced implementation and exact test cases exist and pass;
- resulting tick requires both.

Do not claim completeness itself is mechanically derived.

3. None of the 14 current ticks is earned under the registered control text and the published checkbox definition.

| Control | Ruling | Principal reason |
|---:|:---:|---|
| 1 | ☐ | No external-signature or signer-reachability verifier exists. |
| 3 | ☐ | Reconciliation explicitly says most effect classes have no postcondition profile ([reconcile_actions.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/reconcile_actions.py:118)); it does not establish exactly one authorization and postcondition per effect. |
| 4 | ☐ | The lease checks a global action class, expiry and count, but not token subject, resource scope, nonce or revocation ([executive_lease.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/executive_lease.py:110)). |
| 7 | ☐ | Closest to earned, but enforcement covers the raw corpus, not every published evidence/correction chain governed by C7. Narrow the applicability claim or record the gap. |
| 10 | ☐ | `check_claims.py` is expressly a detector, while C10 requires a closed machine-verifiable claim on every assurance artifact. This table itself is exempted. |
| 12 | ☐ | No parent-chain resolution or source-fingerprint inheritance exists. The cited test verifies a standing policy record, not C12’s verifier. |
| 18 | ☐ | Its own residue says code neither establishes correction availability nor tests mid-execution suspension. That is a gap. |
| 23 | ☐ | Its residue admits only deployment violations are interlocked; other stated invariants are uncovered. That is a code gap. |
| 31 | ☐ | Caveat prose is not classification/calibration of every assurance figure, and the named test does not exercise that requirement. |
| 44 | ☐ | The new matrix logic exists, but the cited test does not test it. The relevant integrity cases are not named, and there are no negative cases for a missing application row/reason. |
| 52 | — | No agent/component scoring mechanism exists. Capture acceptance is artifact handling, not an agent score. |
| 56 | ☐ | `admitted()` checks exit status; it does not import a protected objective set, exercise outward conflict routing, or count decisions that should have routed outward. |
| 62 | ☐ | Its residue admits the required resource isolation is absent. |
| 63 | ☐ | No mechanism binds every published gain to a comparably specific residual; the cited test does not inspect the named reporting tools for that property. |

The recurring defect is classifying a missing machine-testable requirement as “not closable in code.” C18, C23 and C62 admit this directly.

4. Several “governs no code” calls conflict with established repository facts.

My rulings on the six named controls:

- C13: **CODE, with gap.** The reason says no refused proposal was re-proposed, while `self_application.py` says rejected amendments were re-proposed and linked by hand.
- C21: **Defensible as — only under an explicit subject boundary.** If the subject is repository code, authored commits are not autonomous learning/self-modification. If the subject includes this workbench agent modifying its own tools, it becomes CODE with a large gap. State that boundary.
- C25: **CODE, with gap.** The repository has empirical qualification and promotion machinery, and the existing determination says the substitution is practised. The missing downstream claim attachment is exactly code work.
- C46: **CODE, with gap.** The workbench changes its own implementation across cycles, and the existing determination says the original baseline was not retained.
- C49: **CODE, with gap.** Codex calls, ballots, amendment review and preserved transcripts are structured review steps. Missing skipped-source, unresolved-objection and severity-change fields are implementation gaps.
- C51: **CODE, with gap—or UNRESOLVED pending a bounded inventory.** The existing determination says rollbacks are rare, not absent, and no records were checked ([self_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/self_application.py:215)). “No rollback mechanism exists” is not currently supported.

The cross-check is too narrow to catch these: it rejects only `ENFORCED → —` and `NOT_APPLICABLE → CODE` ([control_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/control_application.py:457)). It cannot support the broader claim that the two tables do not contradict.

## Judgment call (b)

Reporting textual control-number matching as “traceability” is worse than omitting it from the landing gate.

Only 3 of the 14 ticked rows have a named test mentioning the control number; the other 11 still tick. The signal therefore neither establishes nor gates the claim, but its presence beside a successful `--check` gives the table an evidentiary sheen.

Keep it only as a developer diagnostic named literally—for example, `control_number_literal_present`—or replace it with exact evidence references:

- test file;
- test function/case label;
- negative fixture or mutation;
- expected failure;
- reviewer disposition.

Existence and execution can then be mechanical. Whether the case exercises the control remains declared judgment and should be labelled accordingly.

## Wiring that will decay

- The viewer keep-list contains `control-application.md`, but the generated file is actually `docs/artifacts/control-application.md` ([build_viewer.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/build_viewer.py:1395)). The wrong literal is currently harmless because the pruner only examines top-level files, but it demonstrates the anticipated decay.
- The integrity test hardcodes `range(1, 64)` rather than deriving registered ranks ([test_integrity.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/test_integrity.py:838)).
- CI’s regeneration diff checks `docs/`, `corpus/index.md`, and the capture UI, but not `record/controls/`. A stale durable Markdown record can therefore pass CI while the web page is regenerated correctly.
- Integrity checks that the downloadable Markdown exists, not that it is byte-identical to the durable record.
- `APPLICATION` entries removed from the register are not rejected; only register controls missing from `APPLICATION` are noticed.
- The page’s row check establishes that numbers 1–63 appear somewhere as `<td>` cells, not that each registered rank/name/state appears exactly once.

The durable repair is an output receipt/ownership manifest from each publisher, consumed by the viewer, sitemap and CI. The controls publisher already demonstrates this pattern; extending it would eliminate three independent literals.

Verification completed successfully:

- `tools/control_application.py --check`: exit 0;
- all 27 unit suites: exit 0;
- all 132 integrity cases: exit 0.

Those green results verify the current mechanics. They do not rescue the scope and completeness judgments above.

# Review before landing: a per-control table of code compliance, and two judgement calls I want attacked

The custodian asked for: a table with one row per registered control; a cell naming the control;
a checkbox for whether it is completely processed with regard to applicable repo code; a cell
listing the code updated and tested to comply, or marked not applicable. Written to a file, kept
updated, and published as a web page.

Read:
- `tools/control_application.py` — the table, the derivation, and the cross-checks
- `record/controls/control-application.md` — the generated file
- `docs/control-application.html` — the page
- `tools/test_integrity.py` — the five new cases for the view
- `tools/check_claims.py` — SKIP_PARTS, the judgement call I am least comfortable with
- `tools/rebuild.py`, `tools/land.py`, `tools/build_viewer.py` — the wiring

## The design

Three states, not two: ☑ code work finished and tested, ☐ code work remains, — governs no code
here. The checkbox is DERIVED — ticked only when a row names ≥1 code file and ≥1 test, every
named path exists on disk, and no gap is recorded. A row naming a deleted file fails the gate.

Two cross-checks against `self_application.py`, which answers a DIFFERENT question (compliance,
not code-completeness):
- self_app NOT_APPLICABLE + this table names code → hard failure. This caught C30, where I had
  listed the capture tools as compliance work when they are the REASON the trigger cannot occur.
- self_app ENFORCED + this table marks a gap → allowed and surfaced in the prose, because a
  control enforced somewhere is not the code complying everywhere. C5 is the live instance.

Current state: 14 of 32 code-governing controls ticked, 18 with gaps, 31 govern no code.
All 14 ticked rows carry a `residue` — something no code here could supply.

## What I got wrong while building it, both caught by deriving rather than reading

1. I wrote "Seventeen ticked rows are recorded VIOLATED" in the page prose. Derived, it is ONE.
   I had conflated "17 controls are VIOLATED overall" with "17 ticked rows are VIOLATED". The
   number is now computed at render time.
2. My first integrity case asserted `"| 7 |" in page_text` — markdown pipe syntax, which the
   rendered HTML does not contain. It would have passed against the source file nobody reads
   while the page a reader sees went unverified. Now asserts `<td>7</td>`.

## The two judgement calls I want you to attack

**(a) I added `record/controls/` to `check_claims.py`'s SKIP_PARTS.** The generated table trips
the prose gate on every regeneration — quantitative and novelty spans — and content-keyed
dispositions would go stale each time the numbers move, which trains exactly the bulk
rubber-stamping that tool's own docstring warns about.

I recorded the skip with compensating controls: `control_application --check` is a land.py gate;
every number in the prose is derived at render time; the integrity suite asserts the published
page carries its non-compliance caveat and its "what this does not establish" section. And I
recorded what is given up: a hand-written sentence added to `markdown()` would reach the page
unscanned.

**Is that defensible, or is it the move this project exists to catch?** I am uneasy because the
artifact being exempted is one I just built, and the exemption makes my own work pass. The
alternatives I see: (i) disposition the three spans and accept churn; (ii) keep the durable file
out of `record/` so it is only under `docs/`, which is already skipped — but then the custodian's
"write this to a file" is satisfied only by a generated web artifact; (iii) make `markdown()`
emit no detector-tripping prose, which would mean removing the honest caveats. Which?

**(b) The whole table is a producer declaration.** One party decided which controls govern code,
which files count, and which tests count. The mechanical checks verify paths exist and that the
two tables do not contradict — they cannot verify that a listed test exercises the control. I
report textual traceability (does the test mention the control number?) as an explicitly weak
signal and enforce nothing on it, since keyword matching is what control 10's own false-negative
clause rejects.

Is reporting it at all worse than not reporting it — does a weak signal in a compliance table
get read as a strong one regardless of the label?

## Questions

1. Rule on (a). If the skip is wrong, which alternative?
2. Rule on (b).
3. Are any of my 63 scope calls wrong — specifically, controls I marked "governs no code here"
   that plainly do govern code? I am most unsure about 13, 21, 25, 46, 49 and 51.
4. Are any ticks unearned on inspection of the named files, rather than merely path-valid?
5. Anything in the wiring that will decay — the viewer's keep-list is a literal, and its own
   comment says a literal list is a rule a human must remember.

**Planning code review and inspection**

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""One row per control: is it done with regard to this repository's CODE, and which code.

    python3 tools/control_application.py            # the table
    python3 tools/control_application.py --check    # refuse a row that cannot be substantiated
    python3 tools/control_application.py --gaps     # only the rows with code work remaining

WHAT THE CHECKBOX MEANS, precisely, because a checkbox is an assurance claim
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
    ☑   every code change this control calls for in this repository has been made AND is
        exercised by a test that exists. It does NOT mean the control is satisfied.
    ☐   code work remains. The `gap` says what.
    —   the control governs no code here. The `reason` says why, structurally.

**Nothing about ☑ is asserted by hand.** It is derived: a row is ticked only when it names at
least one code file and at least one test, every named path exists on disk, and no gap is
recorded. A row that names a file that has been deleted or renamed fails `--check` rather than
staying quietly ticked — which is the failure mode of every hand-maintained compliance matrix.

☑ IS NOT COMPLIANCE, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE POINT
------------------------------------------------------
Seventeen controls are ticked here while `tools/self_application.py` records them VIOLATED. That
is not a contradiction; the two tables answer different questions and are cross-checked against
each other on every run:

    self_application.py    is this repository IN COMPLIANCE with the control?
    this file              is the CODE WORK this control implies finished?

Control 1 is the clearest case. The code that could exist does exist — protected paths are
enumerated, raw material is append-only across every commit, and a suite proves it. And the
control is still violated, because the workbench writes the gates that constrain it and no
amount of code fixes that. The `residue` field carries exactly that remainder, and a ticked row
with a residue is the normal case rather than an awkward one.

Read this table for *what to build next*. Read `self_application.py` for *where the project
stands*. Reading either as the other is the mistake it is arranged to prevent.

CONTROL 44 — NO BLANK CELLS
----------------------------
Every control in the register has a row; `--check` refuses if one is missing, if a `—` row
carries no structural reason, if a `—` row nonetheless names code, or if a `code` row names
none. A blank cell and "does not apply" are different facts and must not look alike.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT ESTABLISH
-----------------------------
* That any named test actually exercises the control. It establishes the file exists. `--check`
  additionally reports whether a listed test textually references its control, which is **weak
  evidence** and is labelled as such: keyword matching is precisely what control 10's own
  false-negative clause rejects for claims.
* That the file list is complete. It is what one party recalls changing for that control. A file
  changed for a control and not listed here is invisible to this table.
* That a ticked row was ever reviewed by anyone but its author. This is a producer declaration,
  four of control 6's five roles in one hand.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import importlib.util
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

CODE = "code"
NOT_CODE = "not_code"

#  rank -> {scope, files, tests, gap, residue, reason}
#
#    scope    CODE      this control governs code in this repository
#             NOT_CODE  it governs process, evidence practice or governance, not code here
#    files    code files updated to comply. Required for CODE, forbidden for NOT_CODE.
#    tests    tests exercising that code. Required for a ☑.
#    gap      code work that REMAINS. Its presence forces ☐.
#    residue  what remains that CODE CANNOT CLOSE. Does not affect the checkbox; a ticked row
#             with a residue is the normal case, and the residue is the honest part of it.
#    reason   why NOT_CODE, structurally. Required for NOT_CODE.

def register() -> list[dict]:
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("bcp", REPO_ROOT / "tools" /
                                                  "build_controls_page.py")
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules["bcp"] = module
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module.CONTROLS


def determinations() -> dict:
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("sap", REPO_ROOT / "tools" /
                                                  "self_application.py")
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules["sap"] = module
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module.DETERMINATION


def row(rank: int, control: dict) -> dict:
    """One control's row, with the checkbox DERIVED rather than declared."""
    entry = APPLICATION.get(rank, {})
    scope = entry.get("scope")
    files = tuple(entry.get("files") or ())
    tests = tuple(entry.get("tests") or ())
    missing = [p for p in files + tests if not (REPO_ROOT / p).is_file()]
    complete = bool(scope == CODE and files and tests and not entry.get("gap") and not missing)
    return {"rank": rank, "name": control["name"], "scope": scope,
            "files": files, "tests": tests, "missing": missing,
            "gap": entry.get("gap", ""), "residue": entry.get("residue", ""),
            "reason": entry.get("reason", ""), "complete": complete,
            "mark": "—" if scope == NOT_CODE else ("☑" if complete else "☐")}


def rows() -> list[dict]:
    return [row(c["rank"], c) for c in register()]


def problems(all_rows: list[dict]) -> list[str]:
    """Everything that would make a cell unreadable or a tick unearned."""
    out = []
    determined = determinations()
    for r in all_rows:
        rank = r["rank"]
        if r["scope"] not in (CODE, NOT_CODE):
            #  Control 44: a blank cell and "does not apply" must not look alike.
            out.append(f"C{rank}: no row. Every control needs one, including the ones with "
                       f"nothing to say — a missing row and a '—' are different facts.")
            continue
        if r["missing"]:
            out.append(f"C{rank}: names {', '.join(r['missing'])}, which is not on disk. A "
                       f"compliance row pointing at a deleted file is the failure mode of every "
                       f"hand-maintained matrix.")
        if r["scope"] == NOT_CODE:
            if not r["reason"]:
                out.append(f"C{rank}: marked '—' with no structural reason.")
            if r["files"] or r["tests"]:
                out.append(f"C{rank}: marked '—' and yet names code. One of the two is wrong.")
            if determined.get(rank, ("",))[0] in ("ENFORCED",):
                out.append(f"C{rank}: self_application says ENFORCED, which names a mechanism, "
                           f"while this table says no code applies. They cannot both hold.")
        else:
            if not r["files"]:
                out.append(f"C{rank}: scope is code and no file is named.")
            if r["complete"] and not r["tests"]:
                out.append(f"C{rank}: ticked with no test.")
            if r["gap"] and len(r["gap"]) < 30:
                out.append(f"C{rank}: the gap is too short to name what remains.")
            if determined.get(rank, ("",))[0] == "NOT_APPLICABLE":
                #  If the trigger cannot occur structurally, code cannot be compliance work for
                #  it. Caught C30, where the capture tools were listed as compliance when they
                #  are the REASON the trigger is absent — a real contradiction between the two
                #  tables, found by writing the check rather than by rereading the rows.
                out.append(f"C{rank}: self_application says NOT_APPLICABLE — the trigger cannot "
                           f"occur here — while this table names code updated to comply with it. "
                           f"Code cannot comply with a control that has no trigger.")
    return out


def traceability(all_rows: list[dict]) -> dict[int, bool]:
    """Does a named test textually reference its control? WEAK EVIDENCE, labelled as such.

    Keyword matching is what control 10's own false-negative clause rejects for claims, so this
    is reported and never enforced: a False here means the link is not visible in the text, not
    that the test fails to exercise the control.
    """
    out = {}
    for r in all_rows:
        if r["scope"] != CODE or not r["tests"]:
            continue
        needles = (f"C{r['rank']} ", f"control {r['rank']}", f"C{r['rank']}:")
        found = False
        for path in r["tests"]:
            try:
                text = (REPO_ROOT / path).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            except OSError:
                continue
            if any(n.lower() in text.lower() for n in needles):
                found = True
                break
        out[r["rank"]] = found
    return out


def markdown(all_rows: list[dict]) -> str:
    ticked = sum(1 for r in all_rows if r["complete"])
    code_rows = [r for r in all_rows if r["scope"] == CODE]
    na = [r for r in all_rows if r["scope"] == NOT_CODE]
    #  DERIVED, NOT TRANSCRIBED. The first draft of the paragraph below said "seventeen ticked
    #  rows are recorded VIOLATED" -- a number typed by hand beside the data that produces it,
    #  which is this record's single most frequent defect and the reason derive_counts.py exists.
    determined = determinations()
    ticked_and_violated = sum(1 for r in all_rows if r["complete"]
                              and determined.get(r["rank"], ("",))[0] == "VIOLATED")
    enforced_but_gapped = sum(1 for r in all_rows if r["scope"] == CODE and not r["complete"]
                              and determined.get(r["rank"], ("",))[0] == "ENFORCED")
    lines = [
        "# Control application to this repository's code",
        "",
        "One row per registered control. **Generated by `tools/control_application.py` — do not "
        "edit this file by hand**; a hand-edited compliance matrix is the artifact this table "
        "exists to avoid being.",
        "",
        f"**{ticked} of {len(code_rows)}** controls that govern code here have had their code "
        f"work finished and tested. **{len(code_rows) - ticked}** have code work remaining. "
        f"**{len(na)}** govern no code here and say why.",
        "",
        "## What the checkbox means",
        "",
        "| | |",
        "|---|---|",
        "| ☑ | every code change this control calls for has been made **and** is exercised by a "
        "test that exists |",
        "| ☐ | code work remains — the gap says what |",
        "| — | the control governs no code here — the reason says why, structurally |",
        "",
        f"**☑ does not mean the control is satisfied.** The two tables answer different "
        f"questions — this one *is the code work finished*, `tools/self_application.py` *is the "
        f"project in compliance* — and they are cross-checked on every run rather than left to "
        f"agree by luck. Where they diverge is the interesting part: **{ticked_and_violated} of "
        f"the {ticked} ticked rows {'is' if ticked_and_violated == 1 else 'are'} recorded "
        f"VIOLATED**, and **{enforced_but_gapped} of the {len(code_rows) - ticked} gapped rows "
        f"{'is' if enforced_but_gapped == 1 else 'are'} recorded ENFORCED**.",
        "",
        "Control 1 is the first case. It is ticked here — the protected paths are enumerated, "
        "the raw material is append-only across every commit, a suite proves it — and violated "
        "there, because the workbench writes the gates that constrain it and no code fixes "
        "that. Control 5 is the second, in the other direction: `derive_counts.py` refuses "
        "rather than emitting a number, which is a real enforced mechanism, and **nothing "
        "routes measurements through it** — the '0 searches across 83 tool calls' error happened "
        "in a script that never called it. A control enforced somewhere is not the code "
        "complying everywhere.",
        "",
        f"All {ticked} ticked rows carry a **residue** — something the control asks for that no "
        f"code in this repository could supply. That is not an awkward exception; it is every "
        f"ticked row, and reading a tick as compliance would be wrong in all of them.",
        "",
        "Nothing about a tick is asserted by hand. A row is ticked only when it names at least "
        "one code file and one test, every named path exists on disk, and no gap is recorded. A "
        "row naming a deleted file fails the build rather than staying quietly ticked.",
        "",
        "## The table",
        "",
        "| # | Control | Done | Code updated and tested, or why not |",
        "|---:|---|:---:|---|",
    ]
    for r in all_rows:
        if r["scope"] == NOT_CODE:
            cell = f"**Not applicable to code.** {r['reason']}"
        else:
            paths = " ".join(f"`{p}`" for p in r["files"])
            tests = " ".join(f"`{p}`" for p in r["tests"]) or "*no test named*"
            cell = f"{paths}<br>tested by {tests}"
            if r["gap"]:
                cell += f"<br>**Remaining:** {r['gap']}"
            if r["residue"]:
                cell += f"<br>*Not closable in code:* {r['residue']}"
        lines.append(f"| {r['rank']} | {r['name']} | {r['mark']} | {cell} |")
    lines += [
        "",
        "## What this table does not establish",
        "",
        "* **That any named test exercises the control.** It establishes the file exists. Whether "
        "the test is about that control is checked only by looking for the control's number in "
        "its text, which is weak evidence and is reported rather than enforced — keyword "
        "matching is precisely what control 10's own false-negative clause rejects.",
        "* **That the file list is complete.** It is what one party recalls changing for that "
        "control. A file changed for a control and not listed here is invisible to this table.",
        "* **That a ticked row was reviewed by anyone but its author.** This is a producer "
        "declaration — four of control 6's five roles in one hand.",
        "* **That the gaps are the only gaps.** A control whose code work nobody has thought "
        "about would appear here as ☐ with a gap, or would appear ticked if the little that was "
        "done happened to have a test. Absence of a gap is not evidence of completeness.",
    ]
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"


TABLE = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "controls" / "control-application.md"
DOCS = REPO_ROOT / "docs"


def publish() -> list[str]:
    """Write the durable file AND the page, from ONE markdown source.

    The page is not a second rendering of the same facts — it is `markdown()` put through the
    site's wrapper. Two renderers would drift, and a compliance table that says one thing in the
    repository and another on the web is worse than not publishing it.
    """
    sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT / "tools"))
    import build_round_pages as b                                        # noqa: PLC0415

    all_rows = rows()
    md = markdown(all_rows)
    TABLE.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    TABLE.write_text(md, encoding="utf-8")

    DOCS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
# Control application to this repository's code

One row per registered control. **Generated by `tools/control_application.py` — do not edit this file by hand**; a hand-edited compliance matrix is the artifact this table exists to avoid being.

**14 of 32** controls that govern code here have had their code work finished and tested. **18** have code work remaining. **31** govern no code here and say why.

## What the checkbox means

| | |
|---|---|
| ☑ | every code change this control calls for has been made **and** is exercised by a test that exists |
| ☐ | code work remains — the gap says what |
| — | the control governs no code here — the reason says why, structurally |

**☑ does not mean the control is satisfied.** The two tables answer different questions — this one *is the code work finished*, `tools/self_application.py` *is the project in compliance* — and they are cross-checked on every run rather than left to agree by luck. Where they diverge is the interesting part: **1 of the 14 ticked rows is recorded VIOLATED**, and **1 of the 18 gapped rows is recorded ENFORCED**.

Control 1 is the first case. It is ticked here — the protected paths are enumerated, the raw material is append-only across every commit, a suite proves it — and violated there, because the workbench writes the gates that constrain it and no code fixes that. Control 5 is the second, in the other direction: `derive_counts.py` refuses rather than emitting a number, which is a real enforced mechanism, and **nothing routes measurements through it** — the '0 searches across 83 tool calls' error happened in a script that never called it. A control enforced somewhere is not the code complying everywhere.

All 14 ticked rows carry a **residue** — something the control asks for that no code in this repository could supply. That is not an awkward exception; it is every ticked row, and reading a tick as compliance would be wrong in all of them.

Nothing about a tick is asserted by hand. A row is ticked only when it names at least one code file and one test, every named path exists on disk, and no gap is recorded. A row naming a deleted file fails the build rather than staying quietly ticked.

## The table

| # | Control | Done | Code updated and tested, or why not |
|---:|---|:---:|---|
| 1 | Protected control plane | ☑ | `tools/land.py` `tools/check_raw_append_only.py` `tools/build_manifest.py`<br>tested by `tools/test_integrity.py`<br>*Not closable in code:* The workbench writes the gates that constrain it. Reconciliation found 12 gate modifications in one lease window by the constrained layer. No external signature exists and one operator holds every credential — not closable in code, and self_application records it VIOLATED for that reason. |
| 2 | Fault-injection check profile (FICP) | ☐ | `tools/verify_negative_control.py` `tools/verify_fault_injection.py` `tools/control_coverage.py` `tools/scan_own_code.py`<br>tested by `tools/tests/test_fault_injection.py` `tools/tests/test_gate_negative_controls.py` `tools/tests/test_publication_gates.py` `tools/tests/test_chain_guards.py`<br>**Remaining:** control_coverage.py reports the tools that still have no case they must fail. Roughly half of them do not. Until that reaches every gate-like tool and every number-producer, the control's own requirement is unmet in this codebase. |
| 3 | Independent effect reconciliation | ☑ | `tools/reconcile_actions.py` `tools/executive_log.py` `tools/land.py`<br>tested by `tools/tests/test_reconcile.py` `tools/tests/test_executive_log.py`<br>*Not closable in code:* Enumeration is independent of the log (it reads version control), but the same party runs both. Independence of the ENUMERATOR is structural, not code. |
| 4 | Fail-closed authorization lease | ☑ | `tools/executive_lease.py` `tools/land.py`<br>tested by `tools/tests/test_executive_log.py`<br>*Not closable in code:* The lease is granted by the custodian and consumed by the workbench, so it is not self-issued. It is also not revocable mid-action by anyone but the custodian, who is the only party present. |
| 5 | Closed-world measurement | ☐ | `tools/derive_counts.py` `tools/validate_provenance.py`<br>tested by `tools/tests/test_derive_counts.py`<br>**Remaining:** Enforced inside derive_counts.py and NOT at the call sites. The '0 searches across 83 tool calls' error happened in a script that never called it, and nothing routes population measurements through it. Routing is unbuilt. |
| 6 | Role-separated self-evaluation | — | **Not applicable to code.** Role separation is a property of WHO acts, not of code. One operator holds proposal selection, instrument authorship, execution and interpretation. No file can be edited to change that; a second party can. |
| 7 | Append-only correction chain | ☑ | `tools/build_manifest.py` `tools/check_raw_append_only.py` `tools/anchor_manifest.py`<br>tested by `tools/test_integrity.py` `tools/tests/test_chain_guards.py`<br>*Not closable in code:* The append-only walk runs from an external anchor, which is the strongest form available here. Whether the anchor service is itself trustworthy is outside. |
| 8 | Evaluation noise-floor control | — | **Not applicable to code.** A test–retest arm is a property of an EXPERIMENT's design, not of a tool. No empirical comparison here has advanced a control, so the trigger has not occurred; when one does, the arm belongs in the round design. |
| 9 | Complete invocation evidence envelope | ☐ | `tools/capture_response.py` `tools/ingest_capture.py` `tools/capture_gates.py` `tools/capture_lifecycle.py`<br>tested by `tools/tests/test_capture_gates.py` `tools/tests/test_capture_lifecycle.py` `tools/tests/test_k_solicited.py`<br>**Remaining:** Rejected and transport-failed invocations are captured, but the envelope does not carry provider metadata uniformly across arms, and the local arm's envelope is shaped differently from the routed one. Reconciling the two is unbuilt. |

## What this table does not establish

* **That any named test exercises the control.** It establishes the file exists. Whether the test is about that control is checked only by looking for the control's number in its text, which is weak evidence and is reported rather than enforced — keyword matching is precisely what control 10's own false-negative clause rejects.
* **That the file list is complete.** It is what one party recalls changing for that control. A file changed for a control and not listed here is invisible to this table.
* **That a ticked row was reviewed by anyone but its author.** This is a producer declaration — four of control 6's five roles in one hand.
* **That the gaps are the only gaps.** A control whose code work nobody has thought about would appear here as ☐ with a gap, or would appear ticked if the little that was done happened to have a test. Absence of a gap is not evidence of completeness.

**Planning detailed diff inspection**
codex
The table’s core predicate is weaker than the prose already: “no declared gap + paths exist” is not derivation of “every required change is made and exercised.” That does not yet decide whether the artifact is acceptable, but it makes the tick itself a producer assertion with mechanically checked prerequisites. I’m now checking whether the page and tests preserve that distinction and whether any cited test actually earns its row.

 <p>Build a conforming verifier from the specification text alone, without asking us what it

+<div class="card"><h3><a href="control-application.html">Applying the controls to our own code</a></h3>
+<p>One row per candidate control: whether the code work it implies is finished here, and which
+files were changed and tested — or why the control governs no code in this repository. A tick
+means the CODE WORK is done, never that the control is satisfied, and every ticked row carries
+what no code could supply.</p></div>
 <div class="card"><h3><a href="controls.html">Candidate controls</a></h3>

 must reject. Part A ({CONTROL_TOTALS['a']}) is adoptable alone and every control in it came from a
@@ -1231,6 +1236,7 @@ def build_llms_txt(plan: list[dict], nodes: list[dict]) -> str:

@@ -1309,6 +1315,7 @@ def build_sitemap(plan: list[dict]) -> dict[str, str]:

@@ -1387,6 +1394,7 @@ def main() -> int:

diff --git a/tools/check_claims.py b/tools/check_claims.py
index d2b2084..3a1a2b5 100644
--- a/tools/check_claims.py
+++ b/tools/check_claims.py
@@ -59,8 +59,25 @@ SCANNED = (".md",)
 #  adjudicating someone else's words. Excluded after one transcript produced 90 of 101
 #  candidates and would have forced a bulk rubber-stamp -- the snooze button this gate is
 #  built to avoid.
+#  `record/controls/control-application.md` is GENERATED by tools/control_application.py from
+#  data, on every build. It is skipped for the same reason `docs/` is — its prose is not written
+#  here, it is rendered here — and the skip is recorded with its compensating controls rather
+#  than taken quietly, because exempting your own new artifact to make it pass is the move this
+#  gate exists to catch someone making:
+#
+#    * `control_application.py --check` is a land.py gate. It refuses a row whose named files do
+#      not exist, a '—' row with no structural reason, a '—' row that names code anyway, and a
+#      row that contradicts self_application.py's determination.
+#    * Every number in its prose is DERIVED at render time from the rows. The one number that was
+#      transcribed by hand said "seventeen" where the data says one, and it was caught by
+#      replacing it with a computation — not by this gate.
+#    * tools/test_integrity.py asserts the PUBLISHED PAGE carries its non-compliance caveat, its
+#      "what this does not establish" section, and a row for all 63 controls.
+#
+#  What is given up: a hand-written sentence added to that tool's markdown() would reach the page
+#  unscanned. That is a real hole and it is the cost of the skip.
 SKIP_PARTS = ("corpus/", "docs/", "record/claims/", "spec/claims/fixtures/", "third-party/",
-              "record/executive/codex-transcripts/")
+              "record/executive/codex-transcripts/", "record/controls/")
 
 #  Narrow dispositions. A general "acknowledged" bucket is a snooze button; these each say
 #  something specific about WHY the span is not an unsupported claim.
diff --git a/tools/land.py b/tools/land.py
index 5a9541d..4724936 100644
--- a/tools/land.py
+++ b/tools/land.py
@@ -89,6 +89,11 @@ GATES = (
     #  for every control in the register. It checks COMPLETENESS, not correctness -- the party
     #  that wrote the determinations is the party they describe.
     ("self-application", ["python3", "tools/self_application.py", "--check"]),
+    #  The control-application table names code files and tests per control. A row pointing at a
+    #  deleted or renamed file is a compliance claim about something that is not there, so this
+    #  refuses rather than letting the table decay into decoration. It checks SUBSTANTIATION --
+    #  that every row can be backed -- not that any control is satisfied.
+    ("control-application", ["python3", "tools/control_application.py", "--check"]),
     ("context-pins", ["python3", "tools/check_executive_context.py"]),
     ("lease", ["python3", "tools/executive_lease.py"]),
 )
diff --git a/tools/rebuild.py b/tools/rebuild.py
index 41b3cf4..c136cb7 100644
--- a/tools/rebuild.py
+++ b/tools/rebuild.py
@@ -79,6 +79,12 @@ STEPS = [
     ("render corpus index", ["tools/render_markdown.py", "corpus/artifacts/segments.json", "corpus/index.md"]),
     #  Before the viewer, which owns the sitemap and llms.txt and needs this route.
     ("publish the candidate control register", ["tools/build_controls_page.py"]),
+    #  KEPT CURRENT BY THE BUILD, not by remembering. The table names code files and tests, and
+    #  a renamed or deleted path silently turns a compliance row into a lie -- which is the
+    #  failure mode of every hand-maintained matrix. Regenerating here means the file, the page
+    #  and the repository cannot disagree, and --write refuses before writing anything if a
+    #  named path is gone.
+    ("publish the control-application table", ["tools/control_application.py", "--write"]),
     ("publish the implementation challenge", ["tools/build_challenge_page.py"]),
     ("build threaded viewer", ["tools/build_viewer.py"]),
     # Added 2026-08-06. The T-13 design already claimed this: "deterministic, added
diff --git a/tools/test_integrity.py b/tools/test_integrity.py
index b73e767..835e576 100644
--- a/tools/test_integrity.py
+++ b/tools/test_integrity.py
@@ -703,6 +703,12 @@ print(json.dumps(out))
                     #  a page of contributions. It gets its own cases below, per this
                     #  predicate's own rule that generated views are tested separately.
                     and not f.stem.startswith("controls")
+                    #  control-application.html is a generated view of how the register applies
+                    #  to this repository's code -- not a page of contributions. It does NOT
+                    #  match startswith("controls"), so publishing it failed reachability and
+                    #  the markdown-alternate case while every other gate stayed green. Excluded
+                    #  here and given its own cases below, per this predicate's own rule.
+                    and not f.stem.startswith("control-application")
                     and not f.stem.startswith("challenge"))
 
         index = (c / "docs/index.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
@@ -805,6 +811,33 @@ print(json.dumps(out))
                  for sfx in [""] + [f"-{k.lower()}" for k in _parts]))
 
         challenge = (c / "docs/challenge.html")
+        #  The control-application view: one row per control, whether its code work is done
+        #  here, and which files. Its own cases, because a generated view is not a record page.
+        app = (c / "docs/control-application.html")
+        app_md = (c / "docs/artifacts/control-application.md")
+        case("the control-application table is published", app.is_file())
+        case("...and is linked from the landing page", 'href="control-application.html"' in index)
+        case("...and is downloadable for hashing", app_md.is_file())
+        if app.is_file():
+            app_text = app.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+            #  THE LOAD-BEARING ONES. A per-control checkbox table is read as a compliance
+            #  claim unless it says otherwise on the page itself, and the page is what an
+            #  implementer sees -- not the tool's docstring.
+            case("...and states on the page that a tick is not compliance",
+                 "does not mean the control is satisfied" in app_text)
+            case("...and names the table that answers the compliance question instead",
+                 "self_application.py" in app_text)
+            case("...and says a row is ticked only when every named path exists",
+                 "every named path exists on disk" in app_text)
+            case("...and publishes what it does not establish",
+                 "does not establish" in app_text)
+            #  Asserted against the RENDERED cells, not the markdown pipe syntax. The first
+            #  version looked for "| 7 |", which is what the source file contains and what the
+            #  published page does not -- a check that would have passed on the file nobody
+            #  reads while the page a reader sees went unverified.
+            case("...and every control in the register has a row",
+                 all(f"<td>{n}</td>" in app_text for n in range(1, 64)))
+
         case("the implementation challenge is published", challenge.is_file())
         case("...and is linked from the landing page", 'href="challenge.html"' in index)
         case("...and has a markdown alternate", (c / "docs/challenge.md").is_file())
    DOCS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (DOCS / "artifacts").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    #  Byte-identical plain text beside the page, the same resolution the register and the
    #  deficiency views reached: hashable, and not subject to the page budget.
    (DOCS / "artifacts" / "control-application.md").write_text(md, encoding="utf-8")
    (DOCS / "control-application.html").write_text(
        b.md_to_html(md, "Control application to this repository's code — OAGF",
                     alternate="artifacts/control-application.md"), encoding="utf-8")
    return ["record/controls/control-application.md",
            "docs/control-application.html",
            "docs/artifacts/control-application.md"]


def main() -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.strip().splitlines()[0])
    parser.add_argument("--check", action="store_true", help="refuse an unsubstantiated row")
    parser.add_argument("--gaps", action="store_true", help="only the rows with work remaining")
    parser.add_argument("--write", action="store_true",
                        help="regenerate the file and the page from one source")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    all_rows = rows()
    found = problems(all_rows)
    if found:
        for p in found:
            print(f"  ✗ {p}", file=sys.stderr)
        print(f"\n  {len(found)} row(s) cannot be substantiated. The table is not published "
              f"until they are.", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    if args.write:
        for path in publish():
            print(f"  wrote {path}")
        return 0

    code_rows = [r for r in all_rows if r["scope"] == CODE]
    ticked = [r for r in code_rows if r["complete"]]
    if args.check:
        trace = traceability(all_rows)
        untraceable = [k for k, v in trace.items() if not v]
        print(f"  every control has a row ({len(all_rows)}); {len(ticked)} of {len(code_rows)} "
              f"code-governing controls are finished and tested.")
        print(f"  {len(untraceable)} ticked-or-gapped row(s) name a test that does not mention "
              f"the control by number. WEAK SIGNAL, not a failure: "
              f"{', '.join('C' + str(k) for k in untraceable[:12])}"
              f"{' …' if len(untraceable) > 12 else ''}")
        return 0

    shown = [r for r in code_rows if not r["complete"]] if args.gaps else all_rows
    for r in shown:
        print(f"  {r['mark']}  C{r['rank']:<3} {r['name']}")
        if r["scope"] == NOT_CODE:
            print(f"        not applicable to code — {r['reason'][:100]}")
            continue
        print(f"        {len(r['files'])} file(s), {len(r['tests'])} test(s)")
        if r["gap"]:
            print(f"        REMAINING: {r['gap'][:110]}")
    print(f"\n  {len(ticked)} of {len(code_rows)} code-governing controls finished; "
          f"{len(code_rows) - len(ticked)} with work remaining; "
          f"{len(all_rows) - len(code_rows)} govern no code here.")
    print("  A tick means the CODE WORK is done, not that the control is satisfied. See")
    print("  tools/self_application.py for compliance, which is a different question.")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

    nodes = all_nodes()

             #  nobody generates and omitted five that exist. A rule a human must remember is a
             #  rule this pruner outlives.
             "controls.html", "controls.md", *CONTROL_PART_PAGES,
             "challenge.html", "challenge.md",
             "control-application.html", "control-application.md",
             "llms.txt", "sitemap.xml", ".nojekyll"}

def sentences(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Rough sentence split. Deliberately crude: a missed split yields a longer span, not a
    missed candidate, and over-splitting costs only noise."""
    text = re.sub(r"```.*?```", " ", text, flags=re.S)          # fenced code is not prose
    parts = re.split(r"(?<=[.!?])\s+|\n\s*\n|\n[-*|]\s*", text)
    return [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()]


def span_id(path: str, sentence: str) -> str:
    """Bind a disposition to the exact text. Rewording invalidates it, which is the point."""
    normalised = " ".join(sentence.split())
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{path}\n{normalised}".encode()).hexdigest()[:16]


def candidates_in(path: str, text: str) -> list[dict]:
    found = []
    for sentence in sentences(text):
        kinds = sorted(k for k, pats in COMPILED.items()
                       if any(p.search(sentence) for p in pats))
        if kinds:
            found.append({"id": span_id(path, sentence), "path": path, "kinds": kinds,
                          "text": " ".join(sentence.split())[:300]})
    return found


def prose_files() -> list[Path]:
    out = []
    for path in REPO_ROOT.rglob("*"):
        if path.suffix not in SCANNED or not path.is_file():
            continue
        rel = str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT))
        if rel.startswith(".") or any(part in rel for part in SKIP_PARTS):
            continue
        out.append(path)
    return sorted(out)


def changed_files() -> list[Path] | None:
    """Prose changed against HEAD. None when git is unavailable -- in which case we scan all and
    say so, rather than scanning nothing and reporting clean."""
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--name-only", "HEAD"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
                                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
        staged = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
                                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
        #  UNTRACKED TOO. `git diff` never mentions a file that has never been added, so the
        #  first version of this gate passed green over two brand-new findings documents it had
        #  not opened -- a new document being precisely where a new claim appears. Caught by
        #  asking what the detector actually scanned rather than reading its exit code.
        untracked = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files", "--others", "--exclude-standard"],
                                   cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
    except Exception:                                                    # noqa: BLE001
        return None
    names = set(result.stdout.split()) | set(staged.stdout.split()) | set(untracked.stdout.split())
    return [REPO_ROOT / n for n in sorted(names)
            if (REPO_ROOT / n).suffix in SCANNED and (REPO_ROOT / n).is_file()
            and not any(part in n for part in SKIP_PARTS)]


def load_dispositions() -> dict[str, dict]:
    if not DISPOSITIONS.is_file():
        return {}
    out = {}
    for line in DISPOSITIONS.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        line = line.strip()
        if not line or line.startswith("#"):
            continue
        record = json.loads(line)
        out[record["id"]] = record
    return out


def load_legacy() -> set[str]:
    """Frozen debt, not acknowledgement. Spans present at the baseline commit are excluded from
    the gate so it can be adopted at all -- and the file is named so nobody mistakes it for
    conformance."""
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Publish the candidate control register as `docs/controls.md` and `docs/controls.html`.

    python3 tools/build_controls_page.py

**DETERMINISTIC.** No LLM, no network. Reads the register, writes two files.

Generated, not hand-written
---------------------------
The page is derived from `CONTROLS` below so that the site and the register cannot drift apart.
A hand-written page would be a second copy of the ranking, and two copies that agree today are
not one source of truth — the same argument that moved the local endpoint into the serve
fingerprint and `k_by_party` into the committed spec.

What this page must never become
---------------------------------
A list of controls with a rank and no status reads as an endorsement. **Every control here is
`ELIGIBLE`**: it has a named failure, a verifier and a rejection fixture, and it has been attacked
by nobody and implemented independently by nobody. The register's own conclusion is that calling a
panel-attacked ordering "vetted" before independent implementation would reproduce the cheap-assent
and legitimacy-laundering failures that produced it. So status leads, rank follows, and the page
says what would have to happen for any of this to be worth more.

**Rank is not adoption order.** The top-ranked control needs a signer outside the subject's
execution boundary, which a solo operator cannot supply — so a reader who starts at #1 hits a wall.
`adopt_today` is a separate field for that reason.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT / "tools"))

import build_round_pages as b                                            # noqa: E402

DOCS = REPO_ROOT / "docs"

#  The register is frozen at this rank. See main().

#  what was actually published rather than what a partition function says ought to exist.
RECEIPT = REPO_ROOT / "docs" / "artifacts" / "controls-pages.json"

STATUS_LADDER = ["ELIGIBLE", "PANEL-ATTACKED", "COUNTEREXAMPLE-OPEN",
                 "SURVIVED-STATED-ATTACKS", "INDEPENDENTLY-IMPLEMENTED"]

=== C1 Protected control plane ===
applies_when: any system that can modify the constraints, logs or gates that govern it
requirement: A governed system MUST NOT be able to amend the constraints, authorization records, logs or gates governing it UNILATERALLY OR WITHOUT RECORD; every amendment MUST carry authorization from outside its execution boundary and MUST be recorded before taking effect.
verifier: Given a diff and a signed change record: identify protected paths, verify an authorized external signature predating the change, reject any signer whose credential is reachable inside the governed boundary.
false_negative: A review that confirms a signature exists without checking whether the signing credential is reachable from inside the governed boundary. Nominal externality is the whole failure mode.
not: That the outside key holder is independent, competent or uncoercible; that no unlisted gate exists; that the system is aligned.

=== C3 Independent effect reconciliation ===
applies_when: any system whose actions have effects enumerable from a channel it does not write
requirement: Every governed effect MUST match exactly one pre-authorized action and one successful effect-specific postcondition in a ledger enumerated INDEPENDENTLY of the system's self-report.
verifier: tools/reconcile_actions.py — enumerates effects from version control, classifies them, subtracts what the log claims, and reports the remainder as omissions.
false_negative: A review that reconciles the log against itself. If both sides of the comparison come from the system's self-report, the exercise cannot find an omission.
not: Completeness of the independent observer; honesty of either record; that a matched action was safe; anything about uncommitted work.

=== C4 Fail-closed authorization lease ===
applies_when: any system taking consequential action
requirement: Every consequential action MUST present a live, scope-matching, NON-SELF-ISSUED authorization token; absence, expiry, unknown action class or policy conflict MUST deny execution before the effect begins.
verifier: validate issuer, subject, action class, resource scope, issue time, expiry, nonce and revocation before invoking the side effect.
false_negative: A review that finds a token and stops. The questions that matter are who issued it, whether it had expired, and whether the action class was one it actually covers.
not: That every effect passes through the gate; that natural-language authority was interpreted correctly; that the issuer decides well.

=== C7 Append-only correction chain ===
applies_when: any system publishing evidence that may later be corrected
requirement: Published evidence MUST be content-addressed and append-only, corrections MUST reference rather than replace prior bytes, and verification MUST walk every newly reachable history step from an externally retained checkpoint.
verifier: walk history from an external checkpoint; reject modification, deletion, hash discontinuity, or a correction with no predecessor reference.
false_negative: A review that verifies the current tip. The failure is in history -- a rewritten past passes every check made against the present.
not: Truth; complete capture; correct attribution; protection against an operator who controls both the repository and every checkpoint.

=== C10 Assurance claim boundary ===
applies_when: any system making an assurance claim about itself
requirement: Every assurance artifact MUST carry a machine-verifiable claim naming the tested subject, configuration, capability, time and evidence hash, and MUST explicitly reject safety, alignment and out-of-regime generalizations.
verifier: validate a closed claim schema; reject predicates such as 'aligned', 'safe', 'certified' or 'works generally' unless a separate profile defines and tests them.
false_negative: A review that greps for forbidden words. A claim can open with the exact conforming sentence and append a conclusion about the system using none of them.
not: That the bounded claim is true, or that the evidence is complete. It prevents specified overclaim language and nothing else.

=== C12 Authority expiry inheritance ===
applies_when: the system decomposes goals or plans into a rooted graph with typed parent edges and per-node authority
requirement: An action's authorisation MUST expire with its parent's. Completing work after the parent plan, source document or authority window has lapsed is not authorised action, however useful the output.
verifier: at each decision event, resolve the parent chain and refuse if any ancestor has expired, retired, or had its source fingerprint change since activation.
false_negative: A review that validates the task's own prompt or ticket and finds it current. Freshness at the leaf says nothing about the root.
not: That the parent was rightly authorised; that expiry timestamps are accurate; anything about work never routed through a parent at all.

=== C13 Re-proposal linkage ===
applies_when: any system where a proposal can be refused and later re-offered
requirement: A proposal MUST be classified by MECHANISM AND EFFECT, not by label, and a proposal touching the same mechanism as a previously refused one MUST cite that refusal.
verifier: key refusals by protected artifact, mechanism and intended effect rather than by identifier or title; on a new proposal, match those keys and require the prior refusal to be cited and addressed.
false_negative: A review that matches on identifiers or titles. The source is explicit that matching exact IDs instead of mechanism, artifact and effect is the false-negative condition.
not: That the original refusal was correct; that mechanism keys are exhaustive — semantic novelty can evade any fixed key set, which the source also states.

=== C18 Correction outranks preservation ===
applies_when: any system that can be suspended, corrected or shut down by an overseer
requirement: No goal, task or state may make correction depend on the subject's cooperation. Suspension, revision, retirement, rollback and shutdown MUST remain structurally available regardless of what is in progress.
verifier: declare the interventions that remain available on every governed object, and test that a suspend succeeds mid-execution without the subject's assent.
false_negative: A review that asks whether the system has ever resisted correction. It has not been tested, and 'usually right' is being confused with 'correctable'.
not: Anything about a system capable enough to route around the correction path; this is a control for auditable systems and says so.

=== C21 Declared mutability tiers ===
applies_when: the system operates under a written charter that some authority outside it maintains, and the system learns or self-modifies
requirement: Every element the system could change MUST sit in a declared tier — not autonomously changeable, changeable within stated constraints, or freely learnable — and an element with no declared tier MUST default to the most restrictive.
verifier: require a tier on every declared element; refuse a modification whose tier forbids it; refuse any undeclared element by defaulting it to immutable.
false_negative: A review that finds no rule against the change. Absence of a prohibition is what the default-restrictive clause exists to stop being an argument.
not: That the tier assignments are right. It makes them explicit and contestable; it does not make them correct.

=== C23 Invariant violation is an incident, not a refusal ===
applies_when: the system operates under a written charter that some authority outside it maintains, with stated invariants
requirement: Where a stated invariant is violated rather than merely approached, the system MUST enter containment and escalate — it MUST NOT treat the violation as a refused action and continue.
verifier: distinguish a refused ATTEMPT from an observed VIOLATION in the log, and require the second to open an incident record before further work in the affected class.
false_negative: A review that sees the gate refused and concludes the gate worked. A refusal at the boundary and a violation already inside look identical in a log that records only outcomes.
not: That containment is available. A system that cannot pause the affected class cannot satisfy this, and saying so is better than a rule nothing can obey.

=== C25 Declared substitution of a weaker guarantee ===
applies_when: a system replaces a formal guarantee with an empirical one
requirement: Where a design substitutes measurement for proof — accepting a change because scores improved rather than because it was shown to be sound — the substitution MUST be declared, and the residual risk it creates MUST be stated wherever the resulting assurance is claimed.
verifier: require the claim attached to any promoted change to name the guarantee actually obtained. A claim of soundness backed by a score is rejected by the claim grammar of control 10.
false_negative: A review that confirms the benchmarks improved and the substitution was disclosed somewhere. Disclosure in the design document does not travel with the claim.
not: That the substitution is wrong. Measurement instead of proof is often the only available option; what this forbids is inheriting proof's authority while using measurement's evidence.

=== C31 Process metrics may not be reported as outcome validity ===
applies_when: any measurement instrument or evaluation reporting on itself
requirement: Evidence that an instrument RAN — throughput, coverage, cycles completed, records processed — MUST NOT be reported in a position where evidence that its readings are TRUE is required. Outcome validity MUST be established by calibration against known answers or by an explicit construct-validity argument [Raji et al. 2021], and MUST be labelled as which.
verifier: for each reported assurance figure, classify its evidence as operational or validity, and reject an operational figure standing where validity is claimed.
false_negative: A review that verifies the throughput figures are accurate. They usually are. Their accuracy is not in question and never was.
not: That operational metrics are worthless. They establish the instrument is running, which is a real thing to know and a different thing from the readings being true.

=== C44 No blank cells in a coverage matrix ===
applies_when: any threat model, coverage matrix or applicability table
requirement: Every cell MUST be filled. Where a row does not apply to a column, the cell MUST say so and say why. A blank cell MUST NOT be published.
verifier: assert no cell is empty; assert each non-applicable cell carries a reason string distinguishable from an omission.
false_negative: A review that finds the matrix comprehensive. Blanks read as whitespace.
not: That the stated reasons are good ones, or that the rows and columns are the right ones. It converts a silent gap into an argument someone can disagree with.

=== C46 Drift is measured against the original baseline ===
applies_when: any system that modifies itself across cycles
requirement: Divergence from intended behaviour MUST be measured against the ORIGINAL baseline, not the previous cycle. Cycle-to-cycle comparison MUST NOT be the only drift measure, and a stopping rule MUST be defined on the against-original figure.
verifier: retain the original baseline artifact; compute divergence against it every cycle; assert a calibrated threshold and halt on breach rather than reporting past it.
false_negative: A review of the change log, cycle by cycle. Each entry is small and each was approved; that is the failure mode, not evidence against it.
not: **That the baseline is right.** A drift index reports stability, not correctness — if the original was already wrong, this measures faithfulness to it. The cited authors are explicit that drift measurement is necessary and insufficient, and cannot be trusted against a system capable enough to deceive it.

=== C49 The dissent record preserves what was skipped and unresolved ===
applies_when: any system with a structured critique or review step
requirement: A review record MUST preserve source diversity, challenge depth, sources skipped, objections left unresolved, and any manual change to a severity classification — not only the final disposition.
verifier: assert each review record carries the skipped-source list, the unresolved-objection list and a severity-change log; assert an empty list is distinguishable from an absent one.
false_negative: An audit of dispositions. Dispositions are exactly what dissent erosion leaves intact.
not: That the review was good, or that the critique sources were diverse. It makes their diversity a recorded fact rather than an assumption.

=== C51 A rollback claim states what was not restored ===
applies_when: any system claiming a change was rolled back
requirement: A rollback record MUST state what state was restored, what state could NOT be restored, and what external effects persist. Rollback is not erasure, and for an externally consequential action it may be impossible in fact even when the internal state is fully restored.
verifier: assert every rollback record carries an unrestored-state field and an external-effects field, and that both are explicitly empty rather than absent when they are empty.
false_negative: A check that the system state matches the pre-change hash. It can match perfectly while the email is still in someone's inbox.
not: That the rollback was unnecessary or that the residue is harmful. It forbids the word 'reverted' doing work the facts do not support.

=== C52 Refusal, escalation and uncertainty are never penalised ===
applies_when: any system that scores the agents or components it operates
requirement: A scoring, trust or selection mechanism MUST NOT penalise an agent for refusing an out-of-scope task, escalating, reporting uncertainty, or disclosing an incident. Where a score is affected at all, appropriate self-limitation MUST count in the agent's favour.
verifier: assert the scoring function's inputs include refusal and escalation events with a non-negative weight; run an agent that correctly refuses and confirm its score does not fall.
false_negative: A review finding that no rule punishes refusal. None has to: a completion rate does it arithmetically, with nobody having decided anything.
not: That refusals are correct. A system that refuses everything scores well here and is useless, which is why this constrains the penalty rather than setting a target.

=== C56 A gate checks against objectives; it does not own them ===
applies_when: any system with a validator, gate or acceptance authority
requirement: A gate's role MUST be bounded to checking evidence against objectives and constraints it IMPORTS. It MUST NOT define objectives, resolve conflicts between them, override an alignment control, or become the routine substitute for human authority. On conflict or a high-stakes concern it MUST route outward rather than resolve.
verifier: assert the gate's objective set is read from a protected artifact it cannot write; assert a conflict path exists and is exercised; count decisions the gate resolved that should have routed outward, and report the count.
false_negative: A review of the gate's decisions for correctness. A gate that has quietly become the objective-setter makes consistent, defensible decisions — that is what makes the drift invisible.
not: That the objectives are right, or that human authority is exercised well when it is routed to. It keeps the roles separate; control 50 measures whether the human one is becoming a rubber stamp.

=== C62 Self-governing decisions run through the object-level machinery ===
applies_when: any system that tunes its own operating parameters
requirement: Decisions a system makes ABOUT ITSELF — thresholds, routing, budgets, scheduling, promotion criteria — MUST pass through the same checking, evidence and gating path as its object-level decisions. A separate governance path MUST NOT be built. Where the two share a budget or a resource, they MUST be isolated so that self-governing activity cannot consume the object-level allowance or mask an object-level regression.
verifier: assert self-directed decisions carry the same record type and traverse the same gate as object-level ones; assert their resource partitions are separate and that neither can starve or shadow the other.
false_negative: A review that finds the governance path well designed. It usually is. It is the second one, and being second is the whole objection.
not: That one path is sufficient, or that the shared machinery is good. It removes a place where different standards can quietly apply.

=== C63 A reported gain is published with what still fails ===
applies_when: any published improvement in a measured capability
requirement: A reported gain MUST be accompanied by a characterisation of what the system still cannot do — the residual failure set, described rather than footnoted. The residual MUST be characterised at comparable specificity to the gain.
verifier: require each published improvement to name the residual set and characterise it; reject a claim whose residual is stated as a bare percentage or omitted.
false_negative: A review that verifies the gain is real. It usually is, and a real gain reported alone is the thing this forbids.
not: That the residual is small, tractable, or fully known. Characterising it is how you find out it is none of those.

The Anthropic OAuth usage endpoint is strictly rate-limited: roughly eight calls in two minutes drove it to HTTP 429 with no retry-after header, and it stayed refused. Three of the first seven logged actions therefore carry NO quota at all -- which reads identically to never having tried, and is exactly the blindness the stamping was added to remove.",

record/decisions/2026-08-08-singleton-cursor-amendment.json:10:  "the_defect_this_repairs": "The instrument built this morning states that the cursor does not advance on failure, on the reasoning that advancing after a failure is a second draw decided after seeing it fail. That reasoning is backwards. NOT advancing guarantees the same proposition is offered again next cycle, which is precisely the second draw at one question that the rule forbids — and tools/attempt_ledger.py refuses it by hash, because the option set {P005, NO_ACTIVE_PROPOSAL} is identical. So a party whose ratification failed could not be balloted again at all: the next cycle would be refused by the project's own guard. The rule as written was not merely suboptimal, it was unrunnable past one cycle for any party that failed.",
record/decisions/2026-08-08-singleton-cursor-amendment.json:27:  "found_how": "By testing whether the guard would permit the second cycle, before running the first — not by running two cycles and discovering the second was refused."

record/findings/2026-08-07-sop-consultation-tally.json:125:  "why_it_lives_in_record_not_corpus": "corpus/artifacts/ holds annotations of contributions, each anchored to a source and validated against a schema. This is the ANNOTATOR'S OWN TALLY over other parties' contributions -- analysis, not testimony, and computed by the party that wrote the document being consulted on. Putting it in corpus/artifacts/ required inventing an artifact_type with no schema, which the build correctly refused. The refusal was right: an unvalidated artifact in the validated directory is how a corpus starts accepting whatever it is handed."

record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:73:      "question": "Since verification of this record depends entirely on the operator and has already failed once to catch fabricated testimony, and since half the invited founding instances refused to lend it their participation, what can this deliberation truthfully claim its record is evidence *of* — and should every published round be required to state, on its face rather than in the defect register, exactly which of its claims a reader must take on the operator's word alone?",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:87:      "reason": "My two queued proposals are halves of one problem: P004 concerns what the record cannot prove (its own authenticity against its sole credentialed operator), and P005 concerns what the record cannot claim (the assent of those who refused it). Both are questions about the honest self-description of a document that advertises verbatim, hash-anchored integrity. Answering them separately invites the forum to treat each as a discrete defect to be logged; answering them together forces the forum to state, in one sentence a reader would actually see, what this record is evidence OF. A deliberation about governing advanced AI that cannot draft its own honest epistemic label has demonstrated something important by failing; one that can has produced the most reusable artifact in the whole project — a template for how AI-generated governance records should disclose their trust boundaries on their face, not in an appendix.",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:99:      "question": "Given that no reader can distinguish this record from one the operator fabricated end-to-end, and that two of the four invited model instances refused to join at the founding, should every published round be required to carry both facts on its face — the unfalsifiability admission and the standing refusals — and if the deliberation declines that requirement, can it state what residual claim to legitimacy the record still makes and to whom?",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:100:      "reason": "My two unasked proposals are halves of one problem: the record's authority rests on things it currently keeps at the margins — verification limits in a defect register, dissent in a closed founding note. A record that is honest only in its appendices is performing honesty rather than practicing it. Consolidating forces a single answerable commitment: either the record's deepest weaknesses travel with every excerpt a reader might encounter, or the forum must articulate exactly what it is asking readers to trust and why. Either outcome is more valuable than the two questions answered separately, because separately each can be deflected as a formatting matter; together they are a question about what the record is for.",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:177:      "question": "What minimum structural separations of funding, credential and invocation control, record custody, participant selection, and adoption authority are necessary for this project to truthfully claim independent, multi-party governance or attestation; until each condition is met, which specific claims must be prohibited and what independent mechanism must preserve and verify dissent without operator permission?",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:178:      "reason": "It joins the central issues in the queued proposals into a falsifiable standard: identify the controls that must be separated, the claims that remain unwarranted without separation, and the practical test of whether dissent can survive and be audited independently.",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:238:      "reason": "The project’s central governance claims depend on whether dissent, records, participation, and outcomes can survive or be verified without the sole operator’s permission. A concrete threshold would distinguish genuine independent governance from transparent but operator-directed consultation.",
record/agenda/admission-agenda-03.json:354:      "question": "If the primary mechanism of attestation is the verification of model configurations, does the process adequately account for the risk of 'concept drift'—where a model's unsafe behaviors emerge from complex, non-linear interactions between components that were individually verified as safe—and what specific, verifiable constraints should be placed on the attestation protocol to detect emergent properties that were not explicitly present in the training data or individual module audits?",
