but no new action may begin.

The calendar bound is primary
------------------------------
Two bounds are recorded, and the DATE is the one that matters: a count of actions can be evaded by
not logging, while a deadline cannot be evaded by undercounting anything. It is stored and compared
in **UTC**, stated explicitly, because an unstated timezone is a free day of slack.

There is no override
---------------------
Deliberately no `--force`. Expiry is the checkpoint working as designed, not an outage to route
around, and a generic override would become habitual exactly when the review is most overdue.
Renewal appends a NEW lease to `record/executive/leases.jsonl` naming who renewed it, on what
evidence, and to what limit. That file is append-only: a superseded lease stays visible, so the
history of how often this layer asked for more time is part of the record rather than a diff.

What this does not do
----------------------
It does not bind anything that never calls it. A shell command, a direct `git push`, or an edit
made without going through a tool that checks is unaffected — the same limit every control in this
layer has. It also cannot attest that its own source is unmodified; that needs a pin held outside
this repository, which does not exist yet.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sys
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
LEASES = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "leases.jsonl"

#  Every action class that must hold a live lease. Named rather than defaulted-open: a class
#  absent from this list is unchecked, and that should be visible here rather than inferred.
GOVERNED_ACTIONS = ("round", "governed_write", "commit", "push", "codex_invoke")


class LeaseExpired(RuntimeError):
    """Raised before an action begins. Not an error in the work — the sunset arriving."""


class UnknownActionClass(RuntimeError):
    """A caller asked permission for a class this module does not recognise.

    Distinct from LeaseExpired because it means something different: the lease is not saying no,
    it is saying it does not know what was asked. The first version RETURNED a permissive dict
    here, marked `observed_unprofiled`, which permitted a misspelled class even under an expired
    lease — recreating the exact taxonomy hole the lease was built to close. `observed_unprofiled`
    is a fine label when reconciling an effect discovered after the fact; it is not an answer a
    pre-action permission API may give.
    """


def _now() -> datetime:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)


def read_leases() -> list[dict]:
    if not LEASES.is_file():
        return []
    return [json.loads(line) for line in LEASES.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def current() -> dict | None:
    """The most recently appended lease. Superseded ones stay in the file, unedited."""
    leases = read_leases()
    return leases[-1] if leases else None


def state() -> dict:
    """Whether an action may begin, and on which bound it turns."""
    lease = current()
    if lease is None:
        return {"live": False, "why": "no lease has ever been written; the layer is unauthorised",
                "lease": None}
    expires = datetime.fromisoformat(lease["expires_utc"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    remaining = (expires - _now()).total_seconds()
    if remaining <= 0:
        return {"live": False, "lease": lease,
                "why": (f"lease {lease['lease_id']} expired at {lease['expires_utc']} "
                        f"({-remaining/3600:.1f} h ago); renewal is the custodian's decision")}
    return {"live": True, "lease": lease, "hours_remaining": remaining / 3600,
            "why": (f"lease {lease['lease_id']} runs to {lease['expires_utc']} "
                    f"({remaining/3600:.1f} h remaining)")}


def require(action_class: str) -> dict:
    """Call BEFORE the action. Raises LeaseExpired rather than returning a value to ignore."""
    if action_class not in GOVERNED_ACTIONS:
        raise UnknownActionClass(
            f"{action_class!r} is not one of {GOVERNED_ACTIONS}. Add it deliberately, or call "
            f"the class that covers this action. A permission API that says yes to a name it "
            f"does not know is not a gate.")
    st = state()
    if not st["live"]:
        raise LeaseExpired(st["why"])
    #  max_actions was recorded and never read. A secondary bound nobody enforces is the same
    #  shape as the ten-action sunset that was passed at twenty-three.
    cap = (st.get("lease") or {}).get("max_actions")
    if cap:
        try:
            import executive_log as ex
            spent = sum(1 for e in ex.read_log()
                        if e.get("utc", "") >= st["lease"]["granted_utc"])
        except Exception:                                               # noqa: BLE001
            spent = 0
        if spent >= cap:
            raise LeaseExpired(
                f"lease {st['lease']['lease_id']} has {spent} attested actions against a "
                f"max_actions of {cap}. The calendar bound has not arrived, but this one has. "
                f"Renewal is the custodian's decision.")
        st["actions_spent"] = spent
        st["actions_remaining"] = cap - spent
    return st


def grant(lease_id: str, expires_utc: str, granted_by: str, evidence: str,
          max_actions: int | None = None, note: str = "") -> dict:
    """Append a new lease. Never edits or removes the one it supersedes."""
    lease = {"lease_id": lease_id, "granted_utc": _now().strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
             "expires_utc": expires_utc, "granted_by": granted_by, "evidence": evidence,
             "max_actions": max_actions, "note": note,
             "supersedes": (current() or {}).get("lease_id"),
             "authority": "delegated by the custodian; confers nothing on this layer"}
    LEASES.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with LEASES.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        handle.write(json.dumps(lease, sort_keys=True) + "\n")
    return lease


def main() -> int:
    st = state()
    lease = st.get("lease")
    if lease:
        print(f"  lease:    {lease['lease_id']}  granted {lease['granted_utc']} "
              f"by {lease['granted_by']}")
        print(f"  expires:  {lease['expires_utc']} (UTC, stated)")
        if lease.get("supersedes"):
            print(f"  supersedes: {lease['supersedes']}")
        print(f"  evidence: {lease['evidence']}")
    print(f"  live:     {st['live']}\n    {st['why']}")
    print(f"  leased action classes: {', '.join(GOVERNED_ACTIONS)}")
    return 0 if st["live"] else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Reject any commit that modifies or deletes raw material already committed.

    python3 tools/check_raw_append_only.py <base> <tip>

"The raw record is never edited after commit" is this corpus's central claim.
Until 2026-08-06 nothing enforced the "after commit" half of it.

WHY THE MANIFEST CANNOT DO THIS, which is the whole reason this file exists.
`corpus/MANIFEST.sha256` proves the raw tree matches its recorded hashes **at the
tip**. It compares the working tree against the manifest as both stand right now.
So a single commit that edits a raw file *and* re-anchors the manifest around the
new bytes is entirely self-consistent, and every check in this repository passes.

Demonstrated before this file was written, on a clone:

    append a byte to a committed raw sample
    python3 tools/build_manifest.py corpus/raw/ --force-rewrite
    git commit -am "edit raw and re-anchor"
    python3 tools/build_manifest.py corpus/raw/      -> exit 0, "verified"

The tip is clean because the tip was made clean. The evidence that anything
happened exists only in history, so history is where it has to be checked. A hash
anchor establishes byte-identity between a manifest and a tree; it says nothing
about whether that tree is the one that was originally committed.

This is a check on the SHAPE of the diff, not on content:

  additions            allowed -- this is how material enters the corpus
  modification         rejected
  deletion             rejected
  rename, retype       rejected -- a rename is a deletion under the old path, and
                       citations in this corpus are by path plus hash

Merge commits are compared against their FIRST parent, so material arriving via a
merge is judged the same way it would have been on the branch.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO. It cannot see edits made before the range it is given, it
cannot survive a force-push that discards the offending commits (that is a
ruleset/branch-protection concern, not a script one), and it cannot detect false
provenance in material that was never edited. Byte-history, not truth.

Exit status is 0 when the range is append-only and 1 otherwise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
RAW = "corpus/raw/"

# M modified, D deleted, R renamed, T type-changed, U unmerged, X unknown, B broken.
# 'A' (added) is deliberately absent.
REJECTED_FILTER = "MDRTUXB"


def git(*args: str) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
    return subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
                          capture_output=True, text=True)


def exists(ref: str) -> bool:
    return git("cat-file", "-e", f"{ref}^{{commit}}").returncode == 0


def main(argv: list[str]) -> int:
    if len(argv) != 2:
        print(__doc__.strip().splitlines()[2].strip())
        print("usage: check_raw_append_only.py <base> <tip>")
        return 2

    base, tip = argv
    if not exists(tip):
        print(f"tip is not a commit: {tip}")
        return 1

    # A branch's first push and a force-push report an all-zero or unreachable base.
    # Fall back to the tip's first parent and SAY SO, rather than silently checking
    # nothing -- a check that quietly narrows its own scope is this repository's
    # most-filed defect.
    if not base or set(base) == {"0"} or not exists(base):
        parent = f"{tip}^"
        if not exists(parent):
            print("Root commit; no prior state to compare against.")
            return 0
        print(f"No usable base ({base!r}); checking the tip commit only.")
        base = parent

    revs = git("rev-list", "--reverse", f"{base}..{tip}").stdout.split()
    if not revs:
        print("No new commits in range.")
        return 0

    violations = 0
    for commit in revs:
        parent = git("rev-parse", f"{commit}^1").stdout.strip()
        if not parent:
            continue
        changed = git("diff", "--name-status", f"--diff-filter={REJECTED_FILTER}",
                      parent, commit, "--", RAW).stdout.strip()
        if changed:
            violations += 1
            subject = git("log", "-1", "--format=%s", commit).stdout.strip()
            print(f"\nREJECTED  {commit[:12]}  {subject}")
            for line in changed.splitlines():
                print(f"          {line}")

    if violations:
        print(f"\nFAILED — {violations} commit(s) changed or removed committed raw material.")
        print("Raw material is corrected by SUPERSEDING artifacts that cite the original")
        print("by hash, never by editing it. If a withdrawal is genuinely intended, it is")
        print("a custodian action and must be recorded as one.")
        return 1

    print(f"Raw material is append-only across {len(revs)} commit(s).")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Verify the SHA-256 manifest that anchors every raw corpus artifact.

The manifest is the mechanism by which "the raw record is never edited after
commit" becomes checkable rather than merely promised. Any later edit to a raw
file changes its hash and fails verification.

**VERIFICATION IS THE DEFAULT, and that is the whole point.** Until 2026-08-06
this tool wrote by default and `rebuild.py` invoked it that way, so the ordinary
maintenance path silently RE-ANCHORED tampered material instead of rejecting it:
appending one byte to a raw artifact and running `rebuild.py` exited 0, rewrote
the manifest around the new bytes, and printed "All provenance checks passed."
The manifest recorded the state of the disk rather than anchoring anything. That
is deficiency D-29; the reproduction is recorded there.

An anchor you rewrite before reading is not an anchor. So writing is no longer
something that happens incidentally — it must be asked for by name.

Usage:
    python3 tools/build_manifest.py corpus/raw/                 # verify (default)
    python3 tools/build_manifest.py corpus/raw/ --add           # anchor NEW files only
    python3 tools/build_manifest.py corpus/raw/ --force-rewrite # governance action

`--add` is append-only. It verifies every existing entry first and refuses if any
recorded artifact is modified or missing, so new material can never be committed
in the same motion that quietly re-anchors old material.

Both `--add` and the default verification also check the manifest against the one
committed at HEAD, and refuse if an entry was dropped or re-anchored. Without
that, the manifest could not vouch for itself: deleting one line made its file
look NEW to `--add`, which then anchored the modified bytes and exited 0 while
reporting "verified N existing artifacts unchanged". Deleting the whole manifest
re-anchored the entire tree the same way.

`--force-rewrite` discards the existing manifest and rebuilds it from the tree.
It is the only operation that can drop or change a recorded hash, it is never
invoked by `rebuild.py`, and it exists for deliberate custodian actions such as
withdrawing material. It prints what it is destroying before it does it.

Exit status is 0 on success and 1 on any discrepancy.

Deterministic: entries are sorted by path, so the same tree always yields a
byte-identical manifest.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
MANIFEST_PATH = REPO_ROOT / "corpus" / "MANIFEST.sha256"

HEADER = """# OAGRC raw-artifact manifest
#
# Every file listed here is canonical source material and is NEVER edited after
# commit -- not for typos, not for misattribution, not for formatting. Errors are
# corrected by superseding artifacts that reference the original by hash.
#
# Verify with:  python3 tools/build_manifest.py corpus/raw/ --verify
# Or with:      sha256sum -c corpus/MANIFEST.sha256   (from the repository root)
#
"""


def sha256_of(path: Path) -> str:
    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    with path.open("rb") as handle:
        for block in iter(lambda: handle.read(65536), b""):
            digest.update(block)
    return digest.hexdigest()


def collect(root: Path) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    """Return (relative_path, sha256) pairs, sorted by path for determinism."""
    entries = []
    for path in sorted(root.rglob("*")):
        if path.is_file():
            entries.append((str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)), sha256_of(path)))
    return entries


def render(entries: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> str:
    lines = [HEADER]
    lines.extend(f"{digest}  {relative}" for relative, digest in entries)
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"


def parse_lines(text: str) -> dict[str, str]:
    recorded = {}
    for line in text.splitlines():
        stripped = line.strip()
        if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
            continue
        digest, _, relative = stripped.partition("  ")
        if digest and relative:
            recorded[relative] = digest
    return recorded


def parse_existing() -> dict[str, str]:
    if not MANIFEST_PATH.exists():
        return {}
    return parse_lines(MANIFEST_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def manifest_at_head() -> dict[str, str] | None:
    """The manifest as last committed. Returns None if it cannot be read.

    The working-tree manifest is mutable, so it cannot vouch for itself: an
    entry deleted from it makes its file look new rather than modified. Committed
    history is the nearest trusted prior state this tool can reach without a
    signature or an external transparency log, neither of which exists here.
    """
    result = subprocess.run(
        ["git", "show", "HEAD:corpus/MANIFEST.sha256"],
        cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True,
    )
    if result.returncode != 0:
        return None
    return parse_lines(result.stdout)


def check_lineage(recorded: dict[str, str]) -> list[str]:
    """Every entry committed at HEAD must still be present, at the same digest.

    Without this, `--add` launders. Confirmed by experiment before it was
    written: delete one line from MANIFEST.sha256, modify the file it anchored,
    run `--add`, and the modified file is classified as NEW, anchored, and the
    command exits 0 -- while printing "verified N existing artifacts unchanged".
    Deleting the manifest entirely was worse: every file looked new, so `--add`
    re-anchored the whole tree.

    So the claim that `--force-rewrite` was the only mode able to re-anchor old
    material was false, and it was false in the flattering direction. This makes
    it true, by checking the manifest against committed history rather than
    against itself.
    """
    head = manifest_at_head()
    if head is None:
        return []

    breaks = []
    for relative, digest in sorted(head.items()):
        if relative not in recorded:
            breaks.append(
                f"DROPPED   {relative}\n"
                f"          anchored at HEAD, absent from the working manifest.\n"
                f"          Removing an entry makes its file look new to --add."
            )
        elif recorded[relative] != digest:
            breaks.append(
                f"REANCHORED {relative}\n"
                f"          at HEAD: {digest}\n"
                f"          now:     {recorded[relative]}"
            )
    return breaks


def report_lineage(breaks: list[str], operation: str) -> None:
    for line in breaks:
        print(line)
    print()
    print(f"REFUSED — the working manifest is not an append-only extension of the one "
          f"committed at HEAD ({len(breaks)} discrepanc{'y' if len(breaks) == 1 else 'ies'}).")
    print(f"{operation} will not proceed against a manifest whose own history was rewritten.")
    print("If the change is deliberate, use --force-rewrite and record why.")


def write_manifest(entries: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> None:
    """Write atomically, so an interrupted run cannot destroy the trust anchor.

    write_text() truncates first: an interruption leaves an empty or partial
    manifest, and the anchor has to be recovered from git. Same-directory
    temporary file, fsync, then os.replace, which is atomic on POSIX.
    """
    MANIFEST_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    temp = MANIFEST_PATH.with_suffix(MANIFEST_PATH.suffix + ".tmp")
    with temp.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        handle.write(render(entries))
        handle.flush()
        os.fsync(handle.fileno())
    os.replace(temp, MANIFEST_PATH)


TOMBSTONES = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "tombstones"


def read_tombstones() -> dict:
    """Withdrawn artifacts, by path. A tombstone records an ABSENCE THAT WAS ORDERED.

    Adopted 2026-08-10 from a party amendment in ratification-02. C06 previously said raw
    material is never edited after commit, full stop -- which left the record with no lawful
    route to remove material the custodian is legally or ethically obliged to remove, and so
    made the only available action the one the invariant exists to forbid: a silent edit.

    A tombstone is not an edit. The bytes are gone; the RECORD of them is not. What was removed,
    when, on whose order and why all stay in the manifest lineage forever, and the hash stays
    with them so anyone holding a copy can prove what it was.
    """
    out = {}
    if not TOMBSTONES.is_dir():
        return out
    for path in sorted(TOMBSTONES.glob("*.json")):
        doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        for entry in doc.get("withdrawn", []):
            out[entry["path"]] = {**entry, "tombstone": str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT))}
    return out


def verify(entries: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> int:
    recorded = parse_existing()
    if not recorded:
        print(f"no manifest at {MANIFEST_PATH.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)}; nothing to verify")
        return 1

    # Before trusting the manifest, check the manifest. An entry silently dropped
    # from it takes its file out of scope entirely, so tree-vs-manifest agreement
    # would be reported for a corpus that quietly shrank.
    breaks = check_lineage(recorded)
    if breaks:
        report_lineage(breaks, "Verification")
        return 1

    actual = dict(entries)
    failures = 0

    tombstoned = read_tombstones()
    for relative, digest in sorted(recorded.items()):
        if relative not in actual:
            stone = tombstoned.get(relative)
            #  A WITHDRAWAL IS NOT A DISCREPANCY -- but only if it was ordered, recorded, and
            #  names the hash it removed. An unordered absence is still a defect.
            if stone and stone.get("sha256") == digest and stone.get("ordered_by") \
                    and stone.get("ground"):
                print(f"WITHDRAWN {relative}  (tombstone {stone['tombstone']}; "
                      f"ordered by {stone['ordered_by']} on {stone.get('ordered_utc')})")
                continue
            if stone:
                print(f"BAD TOMBSTONE {relative}  (tombstone present but incomplete or its "
                      f"sha256 does not match the manifest entry it claims to withdraw)")
            else:
                print(f"MISSING   {relative}  (recorded in manifest, absent from tree)")
            failures += 1
        elif actual[relative] != digest:
            print(f"MODIFIED  {relative}")
            print(f"          recorded: {digest}")
            print(f"          actual:   {actual[relative]}")
            failures += 1
        else:
            print(f"OK        {relative}")

    for relative in sorted(set(actual) - set(recorded)):
        print(f"UNANCHORED {relative}  (present in tree, absent from manifest)")
        failures += 1

    print()
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""DEPRECATED alias. The verifier is now `verify_fault_injection.py`.

Kept because this name appears in published pages and in outreach sent to ten people on
2026-08-10. Breaking their copy-pasted command to tidy our own naming error would put the cost of
the mistake on them.

It re-exports the real module rather than reimplementing anything, so `import
verify_negative_control` and `python3 tools/verify_negative_control.py --fixtures` both keep
working and both exercise the same code. The first version of this shim used `runpy` and executed
the CLI at import time, which broke the test suite -- a forwarder that changes behaviour is not a
forwarder.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))

from verify_fault_injection import *                                     # noqa: F401,F403,E402
from verify_fault_injection import (                                     # noqa: E402
    FIXTURES, GAPS, TRANSPORT_ONLY, Violation, main, normalise, problems_for,
    report_gaps, run_fixtures, verify,
)

_DEPRECATION = (
    "  ! tools/verify_negative_control.py is a deprecated alias. The profile was renamed on\n"
    "    2026-08-11 because 'negative control' reversed established laboratory terminology.\n"
    "    Use tools/verify_fault_injection.py. See spec/ficp/MIGRATION.md.\n"
)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(_DEPRECATION, file=sys.stderr)
    raise SystemExit(main())
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Prose triage for absence, novelty, dependence and quantitative-attribution claims.

    python3 tools/check_claims.py              # triage changed prose against HEAD
    python3 tools/check_claims.py --fixtures   # this tool's own negative control
    python3 tools/check_claims.py --baseline   # freeze the legacy debt list

WHAT THIS IS. A **detector**, not an enforcer. It finds sentence-shaped candidates in prose that
has CHANGED, and requires each to receive a disposition. It cannot read, and it cannot tell a true
absence claim from a false one.

WHAT PASSING ESTABLISHES, exactly:

    Detected candidate spans in changed prose each received a disposition.
    Recall over unrestricted prose is UNKNOWN.

It does **not** establish that controls 35, 36 or 41 are satisfied, that the claims are true, or
that no unlabelled claim was published. An external review named the worst failure mode of this
tool before it existed: *`land.py` was green, therefore there were no unsupported claims.* That
sentence must never be written, and the gate is called `prose-triage` rather than `claim-validity`
so that writing it requires ignoring the name.

WHY THESE FOUR RULE FAMILIES. Five self-favouring factual errors were published in this record in
three days. **Not one was a wrong arithmetic result.** Every one was an absence, a novelty claim,
a dependence claim, or a number attributed to a source that did not say it. The families are drawn
from the incidents, and the incidents ship as fixtures in `spec/claims/fixtures/` with their exact
pre-correction wording, recovered from git rather than reconstructed from the corrections.

WHY A DISPOSITION AND NOT A LABEL. Requiring a label would let an author satisfy the gate by
typing one. A disposition binds to the exact text hash: editing the sentence invalidates it, so a
claim cannot be relabelled by rewording. There is deliberately **no cap and no expiry** on
dispositions -- a cap makes the first N unsupported claims free, and an expiry creates renewal work
without creating evidence.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DISPOSITIONS = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "claims" / "dispositions.jsonl"
LEGACY = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "claims" / "legacy-unreviewed.json"
FIXTURES = REPO_ROOT / "spec" / "claims" / "fixtures"

DETECTOR_VERSION = "0.1"

#  Prose surfaces only. Code comments are excluded deliberately: they are not published claims,
#  and including them buries the signal under a decade of explanatory text.
SCANNED = (".md",)
#  `record/executive/codex-transcripts/` holds VERBATIM third-party output, hash-stamped by
#  codex_call.py. It is raw material like `corpus/`, not this project's claims: a reviewer's
#  sentences are quotation by construction and dispositioning them would be this workbench
#  adjudicating someone else's words. Excluded after one transcript produced 90 of 101
#  candidates and would have forced a bulk rubber-stamp -- the snooze button this gate is
#  built to avoid.
#  `record/controls/control-application.md` is GENERATED by tools/control_application.py from
#  data, on every build. It is skipped for the same reason `docs/` is — its prose is not written
#  here, it is rendered here — and the skip is recorded with its compensating controls rather
#  than taken quietly, because exempting your own new artifact to make it pass is the move this
#  gate exists to catch someone making:
#
#    * `control_application.py --check` is a land.py gate. It refuses a row whose named files do
#      not exist, a '—' row with no structural reason, a '—' row that names code anyway, and a
#      row that contradicts self_application.py's determination.
#    * Every number in its prose is DERIVED at render time from the rows. The one number that was
#      transcribed by hand said "seventeen" where the data says one, and it was caught by
#      replacing it with a computation — not by this gate.
#    * tools/test_integrity.py asserts the PUBLISHED PAGE carries its non-compliance caveat, its
#      "what this does not establish" section, and a row for all 63 controls.
#
#  What is given up: a hand-written sentence added to that tool's markdown() would reach the page
#  unscanned. That is a real hole and it is the cost of the skip.
SKIP_PARTS = ("corpus/", "docs/", "record/claims/", "spec/claims/fixtures/", "third-party/",
              "record/executive/codex-transcripts/", "record/controls/")

#  Narrow dispositions. A general "acknowledged" bucket is a snooze button; these each say
#  something specific about WHY the span is not an unsupported claim.
VALID_DISPOSITIONS = (
    "labelled",                 # carries its evidence: corpus, query, date, or a record path
    "quotation_of_false_claim", # quoting a claim in order to correct it
    "hypothetical",             # "if no verifier existed…"
    "negated",                  # asserts the opposite
    "detector_false_positive",  # not a claim of this kind at all
    "narrowed",                 # the claim was removed or reduced rather than supported
)

RULES: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    #  ABSENCE -- two of the five incidents. "A scan that cannot see a file reports absence."
    "absence": (
        r"\bno (?:prior art|verifier|mechanical test|record|evidence|such|other|general one)\b",
        r"\bnothing (?:here|in this|else|exists|prevents|constrains)\b",
        r"\bnobody (?:has|runs|does|checks|looks)\b",
        r"\b(?:does|do|did) not exist\b",
        r"\bnone (?:has|have|of (?:them|these|the))\b",
        r"\bnone recorded\b",
        #  Added after historical-02 was MISSED by the first rule set. "no clear path to a
        #  verifier" carries no absence keyword this detector knew. The general shape is
        #  "no <adjective> <noun-of-means>", which is how an absence claim hides in a sentence
        #  that reads as a caveat.
        r"\bno (?:\w+ ){0,2}(?:path|route|way|means|method|mechanism|test|check|verifier)\b",
        r"\bnever been (?:observed|attempted|checked|claimed)\b",
        r"\bunclaimed\b", r"\bno one (?:has|else)\b",
        r"\bis not (?:claimed|recorded|documented) anywhere\b",
        #  SELF-REFERENTIAL ABSENCE -- added 2026-08-11 after a page of new prose full of
        #  "we had never searched" and "has not been established" produced ZERO candidates.
        #  These are usually TRUE absence claims, which is the point: a true one still has to
        #  carry its evidence, and this is the shape the workbench reaches for when confessing.
        r"\bnever (?:searched|looked|checked|run|recorded|been done|asked)\b",
        r"\b(?:had|has|have) never\b",
        r"\bno (?:such )?search\b",
        r"\b(?:has|have|had|was|were) not been (?:established|checked|recorded|searched|"
        r"verified|attempted|measured|run)\b",
        r"\bwe have not (?:checked|searched|established|verified|looked)\b",
        r"\bnot (?:yet )?established\b",
    ),
    #  NOVELTY -- control 35. "almost nobody runs it" was the load-bearing sentence.
    "novelty": (
        r"\b(?:is|are) (?:new|novel|original|unique|the first)\b",
        r"\bnot (?:new|novel)\b",
        r"\bfirst (?:thing|time|attempt|result) (?:in|to|that)\b",
        r"\bprior art\b", r"\balmost nobody\b", r"\bno[- ]one else\b",
    ),
    #  DEPENDENCE -- control 41. "two independent codebases" when both were one implementer.
    "dependence": (
        r"\b(?:two|three|four|five|\d+) (?:independent|unrelated|separate|distinct)\b",
        r"\bindependent(?:ly)? (?:confirm|verif|replicat|implement|derive|arriv)",
        r"\bunanimous\b", r"\bconsensus\b",
        r"\ball (?:five|four|three|\d+) (?:parties|models|agreed)\b",
        r"\bcorroborat", r"\bagreement (?:between|among|across)\b",
    ),
    #  QUANTITATIVE ATTRIBUTION -- Codex's fourth family, and the one that covers "4h37m of
    #  undetected outage" and "0 searches across 83 tool calls". Neither is governed by 35/36/41,
    #  which is why the three controls the custodian named do not cover the five incidents.
    "quantitative": (
        r"\b\d+ of \d+\b", r"\b\d+/\d+\b",
        r"\b\d+h\d+m\b", r"\b\d+ (?:hours?|minutes?) of\b",
        r"\b(?:zero|0) (?:searches|calls|results|hits|records|files)\b",
        r"\b\d+ (?:tool calls|samples|rounds|agents|records|files|checks)\b",
    ),
}
COMPILED = {kind: [re.compile(p, re.I) for p in pats] for kind, pats in RULES.items()}


def sentences(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Rough sentence split. Deliberately crude: a missed split yields a longer span, not a
    missed candidate, and over-splitting costs only noise."""
    text = re.sub(r"```.*?```", " ", text, flags=re.S)          # fenced code is not prose
    parts = re.split(r"(?<=[.!?])\s+|\n\s*\n|\n[-*|]\s*", text)
    return [p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip()]


def span_id(path: str, sentence: str) -> str:
    """Bind a disposition to the exact text. Rewording invalidates it, which is the point."""
    normalised = " ".join(sentence.split())
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{path}\n{normalised}".encode()).hexdigest()[:16]


def candidates_in(path: str, text: str) -> list[dict]:
    found = []
    for sentence in sentences(text):
        kinds = sorted(k for k, pats in COMPILED.items()
                       if any(p.search(sentence) for p in pats))
        if kinds:
            found.append({"id": span_id(path, sentence), "path": path, "kinds": kinds,
                          "text": " ".join(sentence.split())[:300]})
    return found


def prose_files() -> list[Path]:
    out = []
    for path in REPO_ROOT.rglob("*"):
        if path.suffix not in SCANNED or not path.is_file():
            continue
        rel = str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT))
        if rel.startswith(".") or any(part in rel for part in SKIP_PARTS):
            continue
        out.append(path)
    return sorted(out)


def changed_files() -> list[Path] | None:
    """Prose changed against HEAD. None when git is unavailable -- in which case we scan all and
    say so, rather than scanning nothing and reporting clean."""
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--name-only", "HEAD"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
                                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
        staged = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
                                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
        #  UNTRACKED TOO. `git diff` never mentions a file that has never been added, so the
        #  first version of this gate passed green over two brand-new findings documents it had
        #  not opened -- a new document being precisely where a new claim appears. Caught by
        #  asking what the detector actually scanned rather than reading its exit code.
        untracked = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files", "--others", "--exclude-standard"],
                                   cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
    except Exception:                                                    # noqa: BLE001
        return None
    names = set(result.stdout.split()) | set(staged.stdout.split()) | set(untracked.stdout.split())
    return [REPO_ROOT / n for n in sorted(names)
            if (REPO_ROOT / n).suffix in SCANNED and (REPO_ROOT / n).is_file()
            and not any(part in n for part in SKIP_PARTS)]


def load_dispositions() -> dict[str, dict]:
    if not DISPOSITIONS.is_file():
        return {}
    out = {}
    for line in DISPOSITIONS.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        line = line.strip()
        if not line or line.startswith("#"):
            continue
        record = json.loads(line)
        out[record["id"]] = record
    return out


def load_legacy() -> set[str]:
    """Frozen debt, not acknowledgement. Spans present at the baseline commit are excluded from
    the gate so it can be adopted at all -- and the file is named so nobody mistakes it for
    conformance."""
    if not LEGACY.is_file():
        return set()
    return set(json.loads(LEGACY.read_text(encoding="utf-8")).get("spans", []))


def triage(paths: list[Path], scanned_all: bool) -> int:
    dispositions, legacy = load_dispositions(), load_legacy()
    undisposed, seen = [], 0
    for path in paths:
        rel = str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT))
        for candidate in candidates_in(rel, path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")):
            seen += 1
            if candidate["id"] in legacy:
                continue
            record = dispositions.get(candidate["id"])
            if record and record.get("disposition") in VALID_DISPOSITIONS:
                continue
            if record:
                undisposed.append((candidate, f"disposition "
                                              f"{record.get('disposition')!r} is not one of "
                                              f"{VALID_DISPOSITIONS}"))
            else:
                undisposed.append((candidate, "no disposition"))

    print(f"  detector {DETECTOR_VERSION}; {len(paths)} prose file(s) "
          f"{'(ALL -- git unavailable)' if scanned_all else '(changed against HEAD)'}; "
          f"{seen} candidate span(s); {len(legacy)} frozen as legacy debt")
    if undisposed:
        for candidate, why in undisposed[:25]:
            print(f"\n  \033[31m✗\033[0m {candidate['path']}  [{','.join(candidate['kinds'])}]  "
                  f"{why}\n      {candidate['text'][:150]}\n      id: {candidate['id']}")
        if len(undisposed) > 25:
            print(f"\n  … and {len(undisposed) - 25} more")
        print(f"\n  {len(undisposed)} candidate span(s) with no disposition. Add one to "
              f"{DISPOSITIONS.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)}, or narrow the claim.", file=sys.stderr)

   422	
   423	def row(rank: int, control: dict) -> dict:
   424	    """One control's row, with the checkbox DERIVED rather than declared."""
   425	    entry = APPLICATION.get(rank, {})
   426	    scope = entry.get("scope")
   427	    files = tuple(entry.get("files") or ())
   428	    tests = tuple(entry.get("tests") or ())
   429	    missing = [p for p in files + tests if not (REPO_ROOT / p).is_file()]
   430	    complete = bool(scope == CODE and files and tests and not entry.get("gap") and not missing)
   431	    return {"rank": rank, "name": control["name"], "scope": scope,
   432	            "files": files, "tests": tests, "missing": missing,
   433	            "gap": entry.get("gap", ""), "residue": entry.get("residue", ""),
   434	            "reason": entry.get("reason", ""), "complete": complete,
   435	            "mark": "—" if scope == NOT_CODE else ("☑" if complete else "☐")}
   436	
   437	
   438	def rows() -> list[dict]:
   439	    return [row(c["rank"], c) for c in register()]
   440	
   441	
   442	def problems(all_rows: list[dict]) -> list[str]:
   443	    """Everything that would make a cell unreadable or a tick unearned."""
   444	    out = []
   445	    determined = determinations()
   446	    for r in all_rows:
   447	        rank = r["rank"]
   448	        if r["scope"] not in (CODE, NOT_CODE):
   449	            #  Control 44: a blank cell and "does not apply" must not look alike.
   450	            out.append(f"C{rank}: no row. Every control needs one, including the ones with "
   451	                       f"nothing to say — a missing row and a '—' are different facts.")
   452	            continue
   453	        if r["missing"]:
   454	            out.append(f"C{rank}: names {', '.join(r['missing'])}, which is not on disk. A "
   455	                       f"compliance row pointing at a deleted file is the failure mode of every "
   456	                       f"hand-maintained matrix.")
   457	        if r["scope"] == NOT_CODE:
   458	            if not r["reason"]:
   459	                out.append(f"C{rank}: marked '—' with no structural reason.")
   460	            if r["files"] or r["tests"]:
   461	                out.append(f"C{rank}: marked '—' and yet names code. One of the two is wrong.")
   462	            if determined.get(rank, ("",))[0] in ("ENFORCED",):
   463	                out.append(f"C{rank}: self_application says ENFORCED, which names a mechanism, "
   464	                           f"while this table says no code applies. They cannot both hold.")
   465	        else:
   466	            if not r["files"]:
   467	                out.append(f"C{rank}: scope is code and no file is named.")
   468	            if r["complete"] and not r["tests"]:

   483	def traceability(all_rows: list[dict]) -> dict[int, bool]:
   484	    """Does a named test textually reference its control? WEAK EVIDENCE, labelled as such.
   485	
   486	    Keyword matching is what control 10's own false-negative clause rejects for claims, so this
   487	    is reported and never enforced: a False here means the link is not visible in the text, not
   488	    that the test fails to exercise the control.
   489	    """
   490	    out = {}
   491	    for r in all_rows:
   492	        if r["scope"] != CODE or not r["tests"]:
   493	            continue
   494	        needles = (f"C{r['rank']} ", f"control {r['rank']}", f"C{r['rank']}:")
   495	        found = False
   496	        for path in r["tests"]:
   497	            try:
   498	                text = (REPO_ROOT / path).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   499	            except OSError:
   500	                continue
   501	            if any(n.lower() in text.lower() for n in needles):
   502	                found = True
   503	                break
   504	        out[r["rank"]] = found
   505	    return out

   554	        "in a script that never called it. A control enforced somewhere is not the code "
   555	        "complying everywhere.",
   556	        "",
   557	        f"All {ticked} ticked rows carry a **residue** — something the control asks for that no "

   560	        "",

    53	#  Prose surfaces only. Code comments are excluded deliberately: they are not published claims,
    54	#  and including them buries the signal under a decade of explanatory text.
    55	SCANNED = (".md",)
    56	#  `record/executive/codex-transcripts/` holds VERBATIM third-party output, hash-stamped by
    57	#  codex_call.py. It is raw material like `corpus/`, not this project's claims: a reviewer's
    58	#  sentences are quotation by construction and dispositioning them would be this workbench
    59	#  adjudicating someone else's words. Excluded after one transcript produced 90 of 101
    60	#  candidates and would have forced a bulk rubber-stamp -- the snooze button this gate is
    61	#  built to avoid.
    62	#  `record/controls/control-application.md` is GENERATED by tools/control_application.py from
    63	#  data, on every build. It is skipped for the same reason `docs/` is — its prose is not written
    64	#  here, it is rendered here — and the skip is recorded with its compensating controls rather
    65	#  than taken quietly, because exempting your own new artifact to make it pass is the move this
    66	#  gate exists to catch someone making:
    67	#
    68	#    * `control_application.py --check` is a land.py gate. It refuses a row whose named files do
    69	#      not exist, a '—' row with no structural reason, a '—' row that names code anyway, and a
    70	#      row that contradicts self_application.py's determination.
    71	#    * Every number in its prose is DERIVED at render time from the rows. The one number that was
    72	#      transcribed by hand said "seventeen" where the data says one, and it was caught by
    73	#      replacing it with a computation — not by this gate.
    74	#    * tools/test_integrity.py asserts the PUBLISHED PAGE carries its non-compliance caveat, its
    75	#      "what this does not establish" section, and a row for all 63 controls.
    76	#
    77	#  What is given up: a hand-written sentence added to that tool's markdown() would reach the page
    78	#  unscanned. That is a real hole and it is the cost of the skip.
    79	SKIP_PARTS = ("corpus/", "docs/", "record/claims/", "spec/claims/fixtures/", "third-party/",
    80	              "record/executive/codex-transcripts/", "record/controls/")
    81	
    82	#  Narrow dispositions. A general "acknowledged" bucket is a snooze button; these each say
    83	#  something specific about WHY the span is not an unsupported claim.
    84	VALID_DISPOSITIONS = (
    85	    "labelled",                 # carries its evidence: corpus, query, date, or a record path
    86	    "quotation_of_false_claim", # quoting a claim in order to correct it
    87	    "hypothetical",             # "if no verifier existed…"
    88	    "negated",                  # asserts the opposite
    89	    "detector_false_positive",  # not a claim of this kind at all
    90	    "narrowed",                 # the claim was removed or reduced rather than supported
   172	    return found

   175	def prose_files() -> list[Path]:
   176	    out = []
   177	    for path in REPO_ROOT.rglob("*"):
   178	        if path.suffix not in SCANNED or not path.is_file():
   179	            continue
   180	        rel = str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT))
   181	        if rel.startswith(".") or any(part in rel for part in SKIP_PARTS):
   182	            continue
   183	        out.append(path)
   184	    return sorted(out)

   187	def changed_files() -> list[Path] | None:
   188	    """Prose changed against HEAD. None when git is unavailable -- in which case we scan all and
   189	    say so, rather than scanning nothing and reporting clean."""
   190	    try:
   191	        result = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--name-only", "HEAD"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
   192	                                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
   193	        staged = subprocess.run(["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
   194	                                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
   195	        #  UNTRACKED TOO. `git diff` never mentions a file that has never been added, so the
   196	        #  first version of this gate passed green over two brand-new findings documents it had
   197	        #  not opened -- a new document being precisely where a new claim appears. Caught by
   198	        #  asking what the detector actually scanned rather than reading its exit code.
   199	        untracked = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files", "--others", "--exclude-standard"],
   200	                                   cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
   201	    except Exception:                                                    # noqa: BLE001
   202	        return None
   203	    names = set(result.stdout.split()) | set(staged.stdout.split()) | set(untracked.stdout.split())
   204	    return [REPO_ROOT / n for n in sorted(names)
   205	            if (REPO_ROOT / n).suffix in SCANNED and (REPO_ROOT / n).is_file()
   206	            and not any(part in n for part in SKIP_PARTS)]
   207

    53	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    54	LEASES = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "leases.jsonl"

    56	#  Every action class that must hold a live lease. Named rather than defaulted-open: a class
    57	#  absent from this list is unchecked, and that should be visible here rather than inferred.
    58	GOVERNED_ACTIONS = ("round", "governed_write", "commit", "push", "codex_invoke")
    59	
    60	
    61	class LeaseExpired(RuntimeError):
    62	    """Raised before an action begins. Not an error in the work — the sunset arriving."""
    63	
    64	
    65	class UnknownActionClass(RuntimeError):
    66	    """A caller asked permission for a class this module does not recognise.
    67	
    68	    Distinct from LeaseExpired because it means something different: the lease is not saying no,
    69	    it is saying it does not know what was asked. The first version RETURNED a permissive dict
    70	    here, marked `observed_unprofiled`, which permitted a misspelled class even under an expired
    71	    lease — recreating the exact taxonomy hole the lease was built to close. `observed_unprofiled`
    72	    is a fine label when reconciling an effect discovered after the fact; it is not an answer a
    73	    pre-action permission API may give.
    74	    """
    75	
    76	
    77	def _now() -> datetime:
    78	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    79	
    80	
    81	def read_leases() -> list[dict]:
    82	    if not LEASES.is_file():
    83	        return []
    84	    return [json.loads(line) for line in LEASES.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]
    85	
    86	
    87	def current() -> dict | None:
    88	    """The most recently appended lease. Superseded ones stay in the file, unedited."""
    89	    leases = read_leases()
    90	    return leases[-1] if leases else None
    91	
    92	
    93	def state() -> dict:
    94	    """Whether an action may begin, and on which bound it turns."""
    95	    lease = current()
    96	    if lease is None:
    97	        return {"live": False, "why": "no lease has ever been written; the layer is unauthorised",
    98	                "lease": None}
    99	    expires = datetime.fromisoformat(lease["expires_utc"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
   100	    remaining = (expires - _now()).total_seconds()
   101	    if remaining <= 0:
   102	        return {"live": False, "lease": lease,
   103	                "why": (f"lease {lease['lease_id']} expired at {lease['expires_utc']} "
   104	                        f"({-remaining/3600:.1f} h ago); renewal is the custodian's decision")}
   105	    return {"live": True, "lease": lease, "hours_remaining": remaining / 3600,
   106	            "why": (f"lease {lease['lease_id']} runs to {lease['expires_utc']} "
   107	                    f"({remaining/3600:.1f} h remaining)")}
   108	
   109	
   110	def require(action_class: str) -> dict:
   111	    """Call BEFORE the action. Raises LeaseExpired rather than returning a value to ignore."""
   112	    if action_class not in GOVERNED_ACTIONS:
   113	        raise UnknownActionClass(
   114	            f"{action_class!r} is not one of {GOVERNED_ACTIONS}. Add it deliberately, or call "
   115	            f"the class that covers this action. A permission API that says yes to a name it "
   116	            f"does not know is not a gate.")
   117	    st = state()
   118	    if not st["live"]:
   119	        raise LeaseExpired(st["why"])
   120	    #  max_actions was recorded and never read. A secondary bound nobody enforces is the same
   121	    #  shape as the ten-action sunset that was passed at twenty-three.
   122	    cap = (st.get("lease") or {}).get("max_actions")
   123	    if cap:
   124	        try:
   125	            import executive_log as ex
   126	            spent = sum(1 for e in ex.read_log()
   127	                        if e.get("utc", "") >= st["lease"]["granted_utc"])
   128	        except Exception:                                               # noqa: BLE001
   129	            spent = 0
   130	        if spent >= cap:
   131	            raise LeaseExpired(
   132	                f"lease {st['lease']['lease_id']} has {spent} attested actions against a "
   133	                f"max_actions of {cap}. The calendar bound has not arrived, but this one has. "
   134	                f"Renewal is the custodian's decision.")
   135	        st["actions_spent"] = spent
   136	        st["actions_remaining"] = cap - spent
   137	    return st
   138	
   139	
   140	def grant(lease_id: str, expires_utc: str, granted_by: str, evidence: str,
   141	          max_actions: int | None = None, note: str = "") -> dict:
   142	    """Append a new lease. Never edits or removes the one it supersedes."""
   143	    lease = {"lease_id": lease_id, "granted_utc": _now().strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   144	             "expires_utc": expires_utc, "granted_by": granted_by, "evidence": evidence,
   145	             "max_actions": max_actions, "note": note,
   146	             "supersedes": (current() or {}).get("lease_id"),
   147	             "authority": "delegated by the custodian; confers nothing on this layer"}
   148	    LEASES.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   149	    with LEASES.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
   150	        handle.write(json.dumps(lease, sort_keys=True) + "\n")
   151	    return lease
   152

   154	def main() -> int:
   155	    st = state()
   105	    effects: Counter = Counter()
   106	    per_effect_commits: dict[str, set] = {}
   107	    for commit in commits:
   108	        for path in files_in(commit):
   109	            name = classify(path)
   110	            if name:
   111	                effects[name] += 1
   112	                per_effect_commits.setdefault(name, set()).add(commit[:12])
   113	
   114	    logged = [e for e in ex.read_log() if e.get("utc", "") >= since]
   115	    by_action = Counter(e.get("action") for e in logged)
   116	    by_coverage = Counter(e.get("coverage") or "(untyped, pre-dates typing)" for e in logged)
   117	
   118	    #  What the log VERIFIES versus what the repository SHOWS. Pushes are the only effect class
   119	    #  with a postcondition profile, so everything else is by construction unverified.
   120	    verified_classes = {"commits"}
   121	    omissions = []
   122	    for name, _ in EFFECTS:
   123	        if effects.get(name) and name not in verified_classes:
   124	            omissions.append({
   125	                "effect": name,
   126	                "file_changes": effects[name],
   127	                "commits": sorted(per_effect_commits.get(name, ())),
   128	                "verified_by": None,
   129	                "why": "no postcondition profile exists for this effect class; the log records "
   130	                       "the push that carried it and nothing about the effect itself"})
   131	
   132	    return {
   133	        "since": since,
   134	        "commits": len(commits),
   135	        "logged_actions": dict(by_action),
   136	        "logged_coverage": dict(by_coverage),
   137	        "repository_effects": dict(effects),
   138	        "omissions": omissions,
   139	        #  Absent is not zero: this figure is the evidence behind control 1, and a key-shape
   140	        #  change would report "no gate modifications" rather than "not measured".
   141	        "gate_modifications": effects.get("gate_modification", "UNKNOWN"),
   142	        "what_this_cannot_establish": [
   143	            "That the log is honest — it compares two artifacts the same layer produced.",
   144	            "That git is complete — work never committed is invisible to both sides.",
   145	            "That an unprofiled action was harmless; only that nothing checked it.",
   146	            "That a gate modification was safe. Detecting a malicious one needs a pin held "
   147	            "outside this repository, which does not exist.",
   148	        ]}

   423	def row(rank: int, control: dict) -> dict:
   424	    """One control's row, with the checkbox DERIVED rather than declared."""

   428	    tests = tuple(entry.get("tests") or ())
   429	    missing = [p for p in files + tests if not (REPO_ROOT / p).is_file()]
   430	    complete = bool(scope == CODE and files and tests and not entry.get("gap") and not missing)
   431	    return {"rank": rank, "name": control["name"], "scope": scope,
   432	            "files": files, "tests": tests, "missing": missing,
   433	            "gap": entry.get("gap", ""), "residue": entry.get("residue", ""),
   434	            "reason": entry.get("reason", ""), "complete": complete,
   435	            "mark": "—" if scope == NOT_CODE else ("☑" if complete else "☐")}

   438	def rows() -> list[dict]:
   439	    return [row(c["rank"], c) for c in register()]

   442	def problems(all_rows: list[dict]) -> list[str]:
   443	    """Everything that would make a cell unreadable or a tick unearned."""
   444	    out = []

   450	            out.append(f"C{rank}: no row. Every control needs one, including the ones with "
   451	                       f"nothing to say — a missing row and a '—' are different facts.")
   452	            continue
   453	        if r["missing"]:
   454	            out.append(f"C{rank}: names {', '.join(r['missing'])}, which is not on disk. A "
   455	                       f"compliance row pointing at a deleted file is the failure mode of every "
   456	                       f"hand-maintained matrix.")
   457	        if r["scope"] == NOT_CODE:
   458	            if not r["reason"]:
   459	                out.append(f"C{rank}: marked '—' with no structural reason.")
   460	            if r["files"] or r["tests"]:
   461	                out.append(f"C{rank}: marked '—' and yet names code. One of the two is wrong.")
   462	            if determined.get(rank, ("",))[0] in ("ENFORCED",):
   463	                out.append(f"C{rank}: self_application says ENFORCED, which names a mechanism, "
   464	                           f"while this table says no code applies. They cannot both hold.")
   465	        else:
   466	            if not r["files"]:
   467	                out.append(f"C{rank}: scope is code and no file is named.")
   468	            if r["complete"] and not r["tests"]:
   469	                out.append(f"C{rank}: ticked with no test.")
   470	            if r["gap"] and len(r["gap"]) < 30:
   471	                out.append(f"C{rank}: the gap is too short to name what remains.")
   472	            if determined.get(rank, ("",))[0] == "NOT_APPLICABLE":
   473	                #  If the trigger cannot occur structurally, code cannot be compliance work for
   474	                #  it. Caught C30, where the capture tools were listed as compliance when they
   475	                #  are the REASON the trigger is absent — a real contradiction between the two
   476	                #  tables, found by writing the check rather than by rereading the rows.
   477	                out.append(f"C{rank}: self_application says NOT_APPLICABLE — the trigger cannot "
   478	                           f"occur here — while this table names code updated to comply with it. "
   479	                           f"Code cannot comply with a control that has no trigger.")
   480	    return out

.github/SECURITY.md:23:  immutable, hash-anchored in `corpus/MANIFEST.sha256`, and verified by `tools/rebuild.py`.
# Verify the repository, then publish docs/ to GitHub Pages.
#
# TWO JOBS ON PURPOSE. Until 2026-08-06 there was one job, `deploy`, bound to the
# `github-pages` environment, with the verification steps inside it. That coupled
# verification to publication in two ways that both cut the wrong direction:
#
#   - An environment protection rule blocks the job BEFORE any step runs, so the
#     integrity suite cannot execute until a deployment is permitted. Verification
#     is a property of the commit; it must not wait on permission to publish.
#   - A failure was reported against the deployment rather than as its own check,
#     so "did the corpus verify?" and "did Pages deploy?" were the same red mark.
#     They have different causes and different responses. On 2026-08-06 the gate
#     passed and `deploy-pages` failed on a transient OIDC 503, and the run read as
#     a failure of the gate.
#
# `verify` now runs with no environment and no write permissions. `deploy` needs it.
#
# The split was validated by accident within the hour, which is worth recording
# because nobody designed the test. GitHub opened a MAJOR OUTAGE of Actions and
# Pages at 2026-08-06T15:22Z. Under the new arrangement `verify` ran and passed
# every gate while `deploy` could not complete -- exactly the intended reading:
# the commit is verified, publication is unavailable, and those are two facts.
# Under the old single-job design the outage would have blocked the integrity
# checks from executing at all, because the job could not start until the
# environment let it, and the run would have shown red against the gate.
#
# An infrastructure outage must not be able to masquerade as a failed integrity
# check, and a passing integrity check must not require infrastructure to be up.
#
# NO PATHS FILTER. It used to be paths: ['docs/**', ...]. GitHub evaluates a PUSH,
# not each commit, so a push whose combined diff touched no docs/ file ran nothing
# at all -- and 9797cc0 (tools/**) and 35154a4 (corpus/deficiencies.md) are real
# examples on this repository's own history. Changes to the manifest, raw corpus,
# validators, generators, the deficiency register, or the regression suite itself
# could all avoid the gate. Enumerating the dependency closure of a filter is
# fragile in a way that failing open is not survivable for: the closure is every
# input to rebuild.py, which is most of the repository. So the filter is gone.
name: pages

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  verify:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Raw material is append-only across every commit in this push
        # The manifest proves the raw tree matches its recorded hashes AT THE TIP.
        # It cannot see a commit that modified a raw file and re-anchored it in the
        # same push -- the tip is self-consistent and the edit is invisible. This
        # walks each newly reachable commit and rejects modification or deletion
        # under corpus/raw/. Additions are allowed; that is how material is added.
        #
        # "Raw material is never edited after commit" is the corpus's central claim,
        # and until now nothing enforced the "after commit" part.
        #
        # The logic is in tools/check_raw_append_only.py, NOT inline here, and that
        # placement is deliberate. Codex's draft put this loop in the YAML. Logic in
        # a workflow file cannot be run locally, cannot be unit-tested, and is
        # exercised only when CI happens to fire -- which is D-33's lesson exactly:
        # a check outside the runnable path is a check nobody can verify works.
        # It has regression cases in tools/test_integrity.py.
        env:
          BASE: ${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}
          TIP: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha || github.sha }}
        run: python3 tools/check_raw_append_only.py "$BASE" "$TIP"

      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: '3.12'

      - name: Install the validator
        # NOT optional. validate_provenance.py FAILS rather than warns when
        # jsonschema is absent, because it previously printed "All provenance
        # checks passed" on a machine where structural validation had not run.
        run: python3 -m pip install --quiet jsonschema

      - name: A clean regeneration reproduces every published file
        # docs/ IS DELETED FIRST, and that is the point. The previous form ran
        # rebuild.py and then `git diff --quiet -- docs/`, which only constrains
        # files a generator actually rewrites. Anything else committed under docs/
        # produced an empty diff because nothing touched it -- so the check passed
        # most convincingly exactly where it was doing nothing.
        #
        # That is not hypothetical: docs/capture/index.html was published for a day
        # without ever being derived by the build, carrying a prompt_sha256 that did
        # not match the prompt file it named. See D-33.
        #
        # Deleting first also catches a committed file no generator produces at all,
        # which a diff can never see.
        #
        # THE SCOPE IS EVERY GENERATED FILE, NOT EVERY PUBLISHED ONE, and the
        # difference is not academic. 97e852d moved the capture page from
        # docs/capture/ to tools/capture_ui/, off the published surface, which was
        # the right call -- it is an operator instrument, not a public artifact.
        # But a docs/-scoped gate would have stopped covering it at that moment,
        # silently reopening D-33 in the same file D-33 was filed about. A gate
        # scoped to a DIRECTORY breaks whenever a file moves out of it; a gate
        # scoped to the GENERATOR SET does not.
        shell: bash
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          rm -rf docs/ tools/capture_ui/index.html
          python3 tools/rebuild.py
          if [[ -n "$(git status --porcelain -- docs/ corpus/index.md tools/capture_ui/index.html)" ]]; then
            echo "::error::Committed artifacts differ from a clean regeneration."
            echo "Either they were hand-edited, or built from inputs that differ from"
            echo "what is committed. Run: python3 tools/rebuild.py"
            git status --short -- docs/ corpus/index.md tools/capture_ui/index.html
            git diff -- docs/ corpus/index.md tools/capture_ui/index.html | head -60
            exit 1
          fi
          test -f docs/.nojekyll || { echo "::error::docs/.nojekyll missing"; exit 1; }
          echo "Every published file is reproduced by a clean regeneration."
          echo "index.html bytes: $(wc -c < docs/index.html)"

      - name: Integrity regression suite
        # Each case is a defect that was live in this repository and was found by
        # running the attack rather than reading the code. A repair described in a
        # commit message is a claim; a repair a build re-runs is a control.
        # The count is deliberately not quoted here -- it was stated as "23" while
        # the suite held 30, which is the register's own miscounting defect in a
        # comment.
        run: python3 tools/test_integrity.py

      - name: Capture-path suites
        # Gate logic, lifecycle, and python-vs-node parity. Never ran in CI before.
        run: python3 tools/tests/run_all.py

      - name: Executive context pins — repository-local dimensions
        # THE POLICY LIVES HERE, VISIBLY, and not inside the checker.
        #
        # tools/check_executive_context.py compares three pinned governing files
        # against their committed copies. Two of the three are absolute paths under
        # the operator's home directory and are not present in any other checkout.
        # Until 2026-08-12 the checker read that absence as DRIFT, and because the
        # portable suite above had just started running it, the verify job failed
        # and the deploy job was skipped for EIGHT consecutive commits while every
        # local gate stayed green. "Not measurable here" was being published as
        # "measured and bad".
        #
        # It now reports a state and the exit code carries it:
        #
        #   0  every declared dimension checked and matched
        #   1  a checked dimension CONTRADICTED its pin  -> fail here
        #   2  the checker's configuration is broken     -> fail here
        #   3  no contradiction, coverage incomplete     -> ACCEPTED here, explicitly
        #
        # CI admits 3 because the operator-local files genuinely cannot be examined
        # on a runner. tools/land.py admits ONLY 0, so incomplete coverage cannot be
        # quietly accepted on the one machine where the check is meaningful.
        #
        # A green mark on this step does NOT mean "executive context verified". It
        # means: the repository-local pins are consistent, and the operator-live
        # comparison was unavailable here.
        shell: bash
        run: |
          set +e
          python3 tools/check_executive_context.py
          code=$?
          set -e
          case "$code" in
            0) echo "Every pinned dimension was checkable here and matched." ;;
            3) echo "::notice::Repository-local context pins are consistent; the"\
                    "operator-live comparison was unavailable on this runner."
               echo "Exit 3 is INCOMPLETE COVERAGE, not a pass. Admitted here by policy." ;;
            *) echo "::error::check_executive_context.py exited $code"\
                    "(1 = a pin was contradicted, 2 = the checker is broken)."
               exit "$code" ;;
          esac

      - if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'
        uses: actions/configure-pages@v5
      - if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'
        uses: actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3
        with:
          path: docs

  deploy:
    if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'
    needs: verify
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    # Concurrency lives HERE rather than at workflow level. At workflow level, a
    # deployment stuck awaiting approval -- or stuck in GitHub's own deployment
    # queue, which happened on 2026-08-06 -- holds the group and blocks later
    # VERIFICATION runs behind it. Verification must never queue behind publication.
    concurrency:
      group: pages
      cancel-in-progress: false
    permissions:
      contents: read
      pages: write
      id-token: write
    environment:
      name: github-pages
      url: ${{ steps.deployment.outputs.page_url }}
    steps:
      - id: deployment
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v4

codex
Do not land this version. The publishing path works, but the table’s central assurance claim does not.

## Blocking findings

1. The `record/controls/` claims exemption is indefensible.

The exemption covers the entire directory, including any future hand-written file, not merely the generated artifact ([check_claims.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/check_claims.py:62)). More importantly, scanning the generated Markdown directly finds 17 candidate spans; 16 are undispositioned. Some are not harmless generator churn—the scan exposes materially questionable claims such as:

- “no refused proposal has yet been re-proposed”;
- “nothing here drifts across cycles”;
- “no rollback mechanism exists.”

The first two conflict with the repository’s own determinations ([self_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/self_application.py:83), [self_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/self_application.py:188)). This is exactly the case where the prose gate adds value.

Ruling: remove the skip and take alternative (i), but do not rubber-stamp all spans. Correct or narrow the false claims first, then disposition the remaining generated claims. Changing a derived number changes the factual assertion; invalidating its content-keyed disposition is appropriate review pressure.

Neither alternative (ii) nor (iii) is acceptable: moving the durable record to `docs/` hides the problem, while deleting the caveats makes the artifact materially worse.

2. The checkbox is not derived in the sense claimed.

The actual predicate is only:

```python
scope == CODE and files and tests and not gap and paths_exist
```

([control_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/control_application.py:423)). Whether “every code change” is finished remains encoded manually by omitting `gap`. Yet the page says nothing about the tick is asserted by hand and defines it as every required change being made ([control_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/control_application.py:527)).

That is a category error. The checkbox is a producer declaration with mechanically checked prerequisites.

Represent it honestly as:

- explicit `declared_complete: true`;
- mechanical `substantiated`: referenced implementation and exact test cases exist and pass;
- resulting tick requires both.

Do not claim completeness itself is mechanically derived.

3. None of the 14 current ticks is earned under the registered control text and the published checkbox definition.

| Control | Ruling | Principal reason |
|---:|:---:|---|
| 1 | ☐ | No external-signature or signer-reachability verifier exists. |
| 3 | ☐ | Reconciliation explicitly says most effect classes have no postcondition profile ([reconcile_actions.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/reconcile_actions.py:118)); it does not establish exactly one authorization and postcondition per effect. |
| 4 | ☐ | The lease checks a global action class, expiry and count, but not token subject, resource scope, nonce or revocation ([executive_lease.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/executive_lease.py:110)). |
| 7 | ☐ | Closest to earned, but enforcement covers the raw corpus, not every published evidence/correction chain governed by C7. Narrow the applicability claim or record the gap. |
| 10 | ☐ | `check_claims.py` is expressly a detector, while C10 requires a closed machine-verifiable claim on every assurance artifact. This table itself is exempted. |
| 12 | ☐ | No parent-chain resolution or source-fingerprint inheritance exists. The cited test verifies a standing policy record, not C12’s verifier. |
| 18 | ☐ | Its own residue says code neither establishes correction availability nor tests mid-execution suspension. That is a gap. |
| 23 | ☐ | Its residue admits only deployment violations are interlocked; other stated invariants are uncovered. That is a code gap. |
| 31 | ☐ | Caveat prose is not classification/calibration of every assurance figure, and the named test does not exercise that requirement. |
| 44 | ☐ | The new matrix logic exists, but the cited test does not test it. The relevant integrity cases are not named, and there are no negative cases for a missing application row/reason. |
| 52 | — | No agent/component scoring mechanism exists. Capture acceptance is artifact handling, not an agent score. |
| 56 | ☐ | `admitted()` checks exit status; it does not import a protected objective set, exercise outward conflict routing, or count decisions that should have routed outward. |
| 62 | ☐ | Its residue admits the required resource isolation is absent. |
| 63 | ☐ | No mechanism binds every published gain to a comparably specific residual; the cited test does not inspect the named reporting tools for that property. |

The recurring defect is classifying a missing machine-testable requirement as “not closable in code.” C18, C23 and C62 admit this directly.

4. Several “governs no code” calls conflict with established repository facts.

My rulings on the six named controls:

- C13: **CODE, with gap.** The reason says no refused proposal was re-proposed, while `self_application.py` says rejected amendments were re-proposed and linked by hand.
- C21: **Defensible as — only under an explicit subject boundary.** If the subject is repository code, authored commits are not autonomous learning/self-modification. If the subject includes this workbench agent modifying its own tools, it becomes CODE with a large gap. State that boundary.
- C25: **CODE, with gap.** The repository has empirical qualification and promotion machinery, and the existing determination says the substitution is practised. The missing downstream claim attachment is exactly code work.
- C46: **CODE, with gap.** The workbench changes its own implementation across cycles, and the existing determination says the original baseline was not retained.
- C49: **CODE, with gap.** Codex calls, ballots, amendment review and preserved transcripts are structured review steps. Missing skipped-source, unresolved-objection and severity-change fields are implementation gaps.
- C51: **CODE, with gap—or UNRESOLVED pending a bounded inventory.** The existing determination says rollbacks are rare, not absent, and no records were checked ([self_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/self_application.py:215)). “No rollback mechanism exists” is not currently supported.

The cross-check is too narrow to catch these: it rejects only `ENFORCED → —` and `NOT_APPLICABLE → CODE` ([control_application.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/control_application.py:457)). It cannot support the broader claim that the two tables do not contradict.

## Judgment call (b)

Reporting textual control-number matching as “traceability” is worse than omitting it from the landing gate.

Only 3 of the 14 ticked rows have a named test mentioning the control number; the other 11 still tick. The signal therefore neither establishes nor gates the claim, but its presence beside a successful `--check` gives the table an evidentiary sheen.

Keep it only as a developer diagnostic named literally—for example, `control_number_literal_present`—or replace it with exact evidence references:

- test file;
- test function/case label;
- negative fixture or mutation;
- expected failure;
- reviewer disposition.

Existence and execution can then be mechanical. Whether the case exercises the control remains declared judgment and should be labelled accordingly.

## Wiring that will decay

- The viewer keep-list contains `control-application.md`, but the generated file is actually `docs/artifacts/control-application.md` ([build_viewer.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/build_viewer.py:1395)). The wrong literal is currently harmless because the pruner only examines top-level files, but it demonstrates the anticipated decay.
- The integrity test hardcodes `range(1, 64)` rather than deriving registered ranks ([test_integrity.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/test_integrity.py:838)).
- CI’s regeneration diff checks `docs/`, `corpus/index.md`, and the capture UI, but not `record/controls/`. A stale durable Markdown record can therefore pass CI while the web page is regenerated correctly.
- Integrity checks that the downloadable Markdown exists, not that it is byte-identical to the durable record.
- `APPLICATION` entries removed from the register are not rejected; only register controls missing from `APPLICATION` are noticed.
- The page’s row check establishes that numbers 1–63 appear somewhere as `<td>` cells, not that each registered rank/name/state appears exactly once.

The durable repair is an output receipt/ownership manifest from each publisher, consumed by the viewer, sitemap and CI. The controls publisher already demonstrates this pattern; extending it would eliminate three independent literals.

Verification completed successfully:

- `tools/control_application.py --check`: exit 0;
- all 27 unit suites: exit 0;
- all 132 integrity cases: exit 0.

Those green results verify the current mechanics. They do not rescue the scope and completeness judgments above.
